### 2026-09-01b — MESH SUITE PLANNED: gizmos (D1) · world-synced overrides (D2) · user-uploaded models (D3) — four research tracks, a slice ladder MS0–MS6, and the finding that D2 is an ACTIVATION of a backend that already exists

**Owner order (same session, after DBG shipped):** build on U8 in three directions — full
move/rotate/scale gizmo suite per mesh (Blender-style, reversible, originals always shown,
extrude stays) · server-persisted world-shared overrides (login-gated, last-committer-wins,
distinct highlight) · logged-in users upload their OWN meshes (auto ground-fit, D1 tooling
for final fit, UPLOAD HERE modal forked photo|model, format + storage research, per-uploader
delete/hide, everyone can re-edit). **Canonical plan: `MESH_SUITE_PLAN.md`** ·
`mem:project/wip-2026-09-01-mesh-suite-plan`. Four parallel research tracks (88–92 %):
**(1) U8 substrate** — key grammar is `<variant>|<cellUri>|<featureId>` and LWW is structural
(FNV-1a `_id` + bulkSave); contracts.md's `osmId` + "LWW by updatedAt" claims are DOC DRIFT
(fix at MS0). The D2 endpoint/validation/dirty-tracking are WRITTEN AND TESTED with zero
callers; provisioning has never run (both verbs 502); the boot-fetch injection point
(`enrichedBuildings.ts:892`) needs zero engine change — **D2 is an activation, not a build.**
The scalar neutrality rule (`|k−1|<0.005` deletes the row) would wrongly delete a
rotated-but-unscaled row — must become identity-across-all-components.
**(2) Mesh anatomy** — the one writer (`applyFeatureSeats`) is INCREMENTAL and only survives
because Y-translate/Y-scale commute; D1's design is lazy pristine-snapshot + ABSOLUTE
recompose about pivot `(cx+tx, baseY, cz+tz)`, preview on a generalized ghost, commit on
release, `addUpdateRange` for drags. The glTF local frame is gizmo-perfect (+X east, +Y up,
−Z north). Landmines named: one-shot `ensureLocated` (a moved feature re-seats to its OLD
footprint), sphere-only bounds (raycast checks the BOX → unpickable-but-visible), party-wall
edge-CSR collisions, whole-buffer re-upload, pristine `cx/cz` = the checksum. **Stock ion
tiles are NOT feature-addressable — building edits scope to enriched + user meshes; trees
deferred (yaw/uniform-XZ only when taken).**
**(3) Gizmo/formats** — three 0.185 TransformControls (r169+ class) fits: `getHelper()`,
`dragging-changed`→GlobeControls.enabled, and `minY/maxY` clamps ARE the ground/sky rail;
TRAP: GlobeControls raycasts the whole scene and Raycaster ignores `visible` → the gizmo
needs a dedicated camera LAYER; proxy carries an ENU quaternion, space:'local'. **GLB
canonical**; accept glb/gltf/obj/fbx and normalize client-side (GLTFExporter, free texture
downscale); **MeshoptSimplifier ships with embedded wasm → auto-decimate over-budget
uploads**; decoder wasm follows the libraw hashed-asset pattern (no `public/wasm/`). Caps
proposed for ratification: 100k tris / 2048² ≤8 tex / ≤25 meshes / 15 MB raw / 8 MB GLB.
**(4) Platform/storage** — the photo|model fork seam is `DropStep.onFiles`+ACCEPT+a new
store phase (today there is NO file validation anywhere — models add the first server mime
allowlist). **Storage verdict: Option C** — Wix Media holds the bytes (MODEL3D is
first-class), a new `UserModels` collection is the source of truth, R2 stays in reserve (the
Worker is strictly read-only with no auth primitive). ★ Four blocking platform unknowns for
Wix MCP at MS0: public MODEL3D `urlExpirationDate`; wixstatic CORS+Range for
`model/gltf-binary`; per-file cap; GLB ingest readiness (`onFileDescriptorFileReady` has
NEVER been wired). **Governance is an owner decision before D3 ships**: no moderation, no
takedown, no attribution exists today (dated accepted POC risk); plus U8's open vandalism
posture + label field; plus re-bakes silently DROP shared world edits (checksum). Slice
ladder: MS0 ratify+probes → MS1 transform substrate → MS2 gizmo UI → MS3 D2 activation →
MS4 upload pipeline → MS5 placement → MS6 management/world-edit. Backlog **T74**. Next
session starts at MS0 (`MESH_SUITE_PLAN.md` §5 has the recipe).

  as the reason the picture can be trusted) · `spot-limits` (RURAL · no building geometry ·
  the TROPICS rise/set case · nothing above the floor).
- **`target-eclipses`** — five rows desktop / four phone, the year on every date, TOTAL/ANNULAR/
  PARTIAL/PENUMBRAL, the dash for a penumbral's zero umbral coverage, greatest-eclipse time with
  the body's altitude, SETS MID-ECLIPSE / BELOW HORIZON, the live NOW line, and the local-vs-global
  split (solar rows are computed for YOUR anchor; a lunar one is the same for everyone on the
  night side).
- **`target-eclipse-scene`** — the half nobody had written down: there is no eclipse toggle, the
  darkness is derived from the sun and moon positions the scene already draws with. Daylight holds
  through the partials and collapses in the last minute (`ECLIPSE.daylightGamma 0.8`); totality is
  deep twilight, not night (`daylightFloor 0.04`); the corona appears only once the last sliver is
  covered and **never in an annular**; the pink chromosphere hairline; bright stars only
  (`STARS.eclipseRevealStart 0.9`, `eclipseRevealMax 0.75`); the copper moon brightest at the
  shadow's edge (`umbraEdgeLift`).
- **`move-ultra`** — the ULT chip, and the one state it never surfaced: a mid-session flip moves
  everything **except** the shadow rig, so a reload is what buys the 8K map.
- **`trust-detail`** — the four baked regions (Dnipro · Pripyat + the Chernobyl reactor ·
  St Albans · the Khumbu around Everest), which of them carry buildings versus a 30 m elevation
  patch, and that Everest is terrain-only on purpose.
- Goals: *"Score the ground around me for where to stand"* → `bestspot`; *"Watch an eclipse before
  it happens"* → `target-eclipses`.

**FIVE STALE CLAIMS CORRECTED IN EXISTING COPY** — each one was a statement about the live UI that
had stopped being true: `move-deck` said **eight** toggles (ULT made it nine) · `plan-open` said
PLAN and FIND share one window and named a two-segment toggle (it is three: `☀ PLAN · ⌖ FIND IN
FRAME · ◎ BEST SPOT`) · `start-shells` did not list BEST SPOT among what the phone leaves out ·
`target-card` carried the wrong eclipse count · `fpv-walk` never mentioned the deck's **ALTITUDE
encoder**, which is a real way to rise and sink on desktop.

**THE SEARCH FENCE IS THE REAL COST OF A NEW TOPIC, AND IT BIT FOUR TIMES.** New copy re-ranks
existing queries, so `guideSearchGolden.test.ts` fails on additions that are individually correct:
1. **`fpv-walk:"altitude"` went orphan** — `spot-altitude`, whose `where` line is the verbatim
   label `SHEET ALTITUDE`, took the top slot and pushed `fpv-walk` out of the required top 5.
   Deleting my own `"sheet altitude"` alias did **not** fix it; the `where` line alone outranks.
   The fix was to make `fpv-walk` genuinely stronger by documenting the ALTITUDE encoder it had
   always omitted — **the content gap and the ranking bug were the same bug**.
2. **A bare number in an alias reaches nothing** — `"1 m"` returns `[]` outright and `"top 8"`
   returned pure noise. Never author an alias containing a lone digit.
3. **`"re-solve"` stole the shipped golden row `"re-centre" → fpv-map-controls`** through the fuzzy
   ladder. A new alias must be checked against the GOLDEN table's near-neighbours, not only
   against its own topic.
4. **The shipped goal phrase *"Read when the light is right at a spot"* started resolving to
   `spot-markers`**, because "spot" is now a chapter-wide word. Fixed inside the new topic's
   `keys` (`ranked spots` → `ranked cells`) rather than by rewording a goal the owner has seen.

**THE /no-slop PASS FOUND NINE PATTERNS IN MY OWN DRAFT** and the repo's banned-phrase lint then
caught the pass itself: the rewrite introduced *"cannot be scored honestly"*, and `honestly` is on
the intensifier list. Cut: two fake-profound kickers, a rhetorical question in a tip
(*"why is there a #4 over there?"*), a negative-parallelism opener (*"An eclipse is not a layer you
switch on"*), unfalsifiable comparative puffery (*"a block of lines most tools would leave out"*),
a superlative (*"the worst thing this feature could do"*), and two vague quantities that became
plain ones. **The lint walks `keys` too — aliases are authored prose and drift the same way.**

**ONE DEFECT FOUND, NOT FIXED — T71.** `BestSpotPanel.tsx:179-180` tells the user to
*"TURN ▦ 3D DETAIL ON"*. `▦ 3D DETAIL` is the **/m LAYERS** chip; BEST SPOT mounts only from
`index.astro` and is gated desktop-only, where the same layer is the camera deck's **BLD**. The
message names a control that cannot be on screen when it is shown. `spot-limits.tip` documents the
mismatch verbatim, so the string fix and that tip must move together. **T72** records that all four
new subjects ship text-only: `guideContent.test.ts:185` pins `allMedia()` at exactly 13, and a shot
of the heatmap, the shortlist or an eclipsed sky needs a `wix dev` shoot session — it rides T42.

**Files:** `src/lib/guide/guideContent.ts` (+13 topics, +1 chapter, +2 goals, 5 corrections) ·
`.claude/skills/frame/references/tracked-backlog.md` (T71, T72). **Verification tier:
local-runnable only** — vitest + `astro check` + knip. No browser leg was needed or claimed.

### 2026-08-27c — THE TASTE PASS, AND THE LESSON IT COST: dimming the key is only HALF a dusk, because contrast is `direct / (direct + flat)` and 2026-08-27b divided the numerator by five while holding every flat term fixed — the definition of flattening

The owner tested 2026-08-27b and reported four things still wrong: the sun disc "too white and transparent"; the "weird tint, especially on backside of objects" still there, with "mountains just below the sun which should be in complete shadow" reading bright; cast shadows that "should become darker and more global, not just disappear"; and the afterglow still missing. **Every one turned out to be a term LEFT OUT of the first pass rather than a knob set badly** — which is exactly why the first pass could not have been tuned into correctness.

An audit was run first (4 read-only lenses over the low-sun light path, then an adversarial refutation pass; 39 agents). **It paid for itself three times**: it found the largest terrain flattener, which was hiding in the TEXTURE half of the track and which I had not suspected; it refuted an idea of mine (tilting the HemisphereLight toward the sun) by measuring the hemisphere at **0.18 % of a facade pixel**; and its refutation of its own top finding caught a regression I had just introduced. It also cost a methodological lesson: I implemented while the refuters were still running, so several "REFUTED" verdicts are line-number drift from my own edits rather than real refutations — the adversarial phase must finish before the tree moves.

**THE TERRAIN — a measured ratio of 0.969.** At +2° two 30° slopes, one facing the sun and one facing DIRECTLY AWAY, rendered at **0.859 vs 0.833**: the mountain in complete shadow at **96.9 %** of the one in full sun. Four independently direction-blind terms compounded. (1) **`shade = mix(shade, 1.0, photo)` at `photo3dK` 0.6** — the §1a photographic de-grade pulls the ENTIRE shade term 60 % toward white; it alone took the ratio from 1.112 to 1.031, and `photo3dK`'s own docblock lists "shade→1" as part of the raw-Esri de-grade, i.e. the shading half was bundled with the colour half and only the colour half is defensible in 3D. It now rides `directK^3` — exactly 1 at high sun (so the daytime frame the owner LIKES is byte-identical) and 0.066 by +2°; the power must be steep because `mix(x, 1.0, k)` lifts dark values more than bright ones, and a linear ride still left the faces at 0.78. (2) **The ambient half of 08-27b's own direct/ambient split** was a constant times a term that only knows which way is UP — at dusk it was *worse than the `EARTH.dayGradMin` 0.78 ramp it replaced*, because 0.78 was at least reached through `sqrt(sunDot)` on the lit side. It gains a LEVEL and an AZIMUTH. **The azimuth's first form was wrong and the JS twin caught it**: reusing `ftwAirLevel` — the air-light's own lobe pair — gave 0.50 vs 0.44, no contrast at all, because that lobe is the sky's RADIANCE ALONG ONE RAY while a surface integrates the hemisphere around its normal. The cosine-weighted integral of a one-sided sky IS the wrap `0.5 + 0.5·dot(n, sun)`, and its strength is `(1 − directK)^0.5` — physically right and free, since the sky is isotropic at noon (noon provably untouched) and most one-sided at the horizon. (3) The **golden-hour cast**, a bell over SOLAR elevation multiplied into every fragment on the day side — the terrain twin of the `hemiTintK` bug 08-27b fixed for buildings and left in place; now rides `directK · lambert`. (4) The **additive `uFtwAmbDay` floor**, no normal term at all and still 88 % of its +2° value at 0°. **Measured after: 0.685 at +2°, 0.653 at 0°, monotone from 10° down, with the LIT slope falling 0.99 → 0.49 → 0.31** — the ratio was not bought by lifting the shadow side. Pinned by `test/components/globe/duskShadeRatio.test.ts`, a JS twin of the shade chain which also caught a sign error in its own geometry.

**THE CITY — a constant floor 3.6× the sun.** `BUILDINGS.emissiveIntensity` 0.1 on `tokens.land` is a CONSTANT (0.0087, 0.0148, 0.0107) added straight to `outgoingLight` with no albedo, no normal and no sun term: **at a 3° sun a wall pointed straight into the sun receives ~3.6× more light from its own emissive than from the sun**, and it is ~100× the whole HemisphereLight contribution. Measured front:back on a facade before this: **1.28 at 3°, 1.21 at 2°, 1.08 at 0°**. Shipped `buildingEmisCurve` + `buildingEdgeCurve`, both TROUGHS (the emissive floor IS the night look, so it must return after twilight), applied through ONE authority per module so they cannot fight the ghost-mode and FPV-solidity writers that already own edge opacity. The unlit edge strokes matter too — at a 3° sun an edge pixel is **6.4× the entire lit surface of the wall it outlines**. And `moonLight` finally gets a moon-ELEVATION gate: it had none, so a below-horizon moon still keyed every wall facing its azimuth at **82 % of the sun key at 0° sun, 33× stronger in blue** — the ground has gated its moon terms on elevation since S7 and the buildings never did, and that asymmetry is what marks it an oversight.

**THE SUN DISC — additive has no dim-but-solid state.** The impostor was `AdditiveBlending`, so its result is literally `disc + sky` and **dimming it and dissolving it into the sky are the same operation**; 08-27b scaled the additive level down and got what addition promises — a flat pale plate the colour of the sky behind it (measured core radiance 5.00 at 14° → 1.11 at 1.6°, spread uniformly by the flat-topped disc mask). It now carries a PREMULTIPLIED arm as well — the moon's `ONE / ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` triple verbatim, on a different axis (the moon switches day↔night; the sun switches bright-and-additive ↔ dim-and-solid). Three traps, all handled: **`uSolid` is EXACTLY 0 above 6°**, so the blend degenerates to the addition it replaces and the daytime frame is provably unmoved; **ONE coverage scalar** drives rgb AND alpha, because under premultiplied "over" a fragment with rgb 0 and a 1 is BLACK, so a mask applied to colour alone — as all four correctly were under addition — would punch a black bite at the eclipse silhouette and the setting limb (`verify-eclipse` 37/0 is the proof it does not); the **halo stays rgb-only**, or ~14 solar diameters of sky go partly opaque. `discLevelCurve` replaces the `sunExtinctionK × keyExtinctCurve` product (which started at 10°, not "earlier", and whose `ultraExtinctFloor` left the core at 0.028 — about half the sky behind it) and places the `BLOOM.threshold` crossing at ~5.5°, deliberately ABOVE the band he watches. The chroma is FLOORED per channel: raw `solarChroma` at 0° is (1.000, 0.212, 0.005), which is crimson with a dead blue channel, not orange. **Measured after: level 0.771 → 0.115 across 14° → 1.6°, `solid` 0 → 0.95, `haloK` 0.60 → 0.013, tint white → `#ffd6bb`.**

**THE AFTERGLOW — a lerp target dimmer than the term it replaced.** The directional dome arm was blended by `hazeNow × domeTintK`, **capped at 0.3825** — and the legacy omnidirectional band it lerped against rides `max(dayK, hGold)`, where `GOLDEN.fadeInLo` = sin(−12.1°) holds `hGold` at **0.977 at −2°**. Since 0.75 < 0.977, **`mix()` SUBTRACTED**: ULTRA could only ever make the dusk sun-side horizon DARKER than baseline, and the afterglow first exceeds the legacy term at −9.45° where the weight is 0.154 and the band is invisible. Fixed three ways — its own weight (`domeDirK`, so `domeTintK` goes back to being the TINT coupling its docblock describes rather than a master gain on a light source); an ADDITIVE band with `afterglowTauSin` 0.30 (~17.5°) instead of the daytime crest's ~4.3° ribbon; and passed into `ftwAerial`, because the dome was painting a glow the terrain under it knew nothing about (0.49 against a far-field level of 0.22 at −6°).

**CAST SHADOWS** ("should become darker and more global"): `sunLight.shadow.intensity = aboveGateK(sunDot, KEY_GATE)` faded the whole field over sin(3°) — measured **52 % by +2° and 9 % by +1°**, the exact band where a raking shadow is the most dramatic thing in the frame. That loss was recorded on `SHADOWS.fadeBandSin` as owner A/B item **AB4**, deferred for a verdict; the verdict is now in. The band cannot be deleted — it also hides the sun→moon SOURCE SWITCH, which teleports the rig's direction at `minSunElevSin` — so ULTRA gets its own NARROW band (`shadowFadeBandSin` sin(0.6°)) for the shadow field alone, still exactly 0 AT the gate, while the key trough and the moon takeover keep the wide one. The overlay also DEEPENS toward `groundShadowDuskK` 0.88 over the wide band: contrast, never shape.

**ONE REGRESSION FOUND AND FIXED BY THE ADVERSARIAL PASS.** Splitting the shade lift off would have left `moonlit` and `ambient` suppressed by the UNCHANGED colorimetry `photo` 0.6 under a shade that no longer rises — making the ULTRA night ground strictly DARKER than the ULTRA-OFF one and re-opening the S7 "ground jarringly black" condition. Both now ride `photoShade`, which IS `photo` with the chip off and on the /m chart.

**NEW:** `test/components/globe/sunDisc.test.ts` (16) + `duskShadeRatio.test.ts` (6) + `sunDiscArms` CPU twin · `scripts/probe-sundisc.mjs`. **EXTENDED:** `tuning.SKY` (disc level/solidity/chroma/halo curves) · `tuning.ULTRA` (`photo3dShadePow`, `groundAmbientAzK`/`AzPow`/`LevelK`, `shadowFadeBandSin`, `groundShadowDuskK`, `domeDirK`, `afterglowGain`/`TauSin`, `buildingEmisCurve`/`EdgeCurve`) · `sky.ts` (premultiplied disc) · `imageryGround` · `atmosphere` · `glsl.FTW_AERIAL_GLSL` · `buildings`/`enrichedBuildings`/`buildingMaterial` · `StylizedTiles`.

**Gates: vitest 2,210/2,210 (146 files) · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn · `npx knip` exit-0 · `verify-ultra` 28/28 · `verify-ultra-dusk` 21/21 · `verify-eclipse` 37 PASS / 0 FAIL · `verify-rendering-charter` 85/85.** Doc: `rendering/ULTRA_ARCHITECTURE.md` §14. New backlog: **T68** (the "sun behind a mountain" afterglow — honestly NOT shipped, and why), **T69** (the aerial perspective erases contrast past ~5 km regardless of the surface), **T70** (the dome's Mie lobe mixes two frames, ~0.19°).


### 2026-08-27b — THE OWNER'S THREE ULTRA IMMERSION BREAKERS, ALL THREE MEASURED BEFORE THEY WERE FIXED: the shadow box covered **8-35 %** of a mountain frame, the air-light had no LEVEL term so dusk's far field was BRIGHTER than its foreground, and the dark tile grid was the terrain SKIRT sitting in the shadow pipeline on both sides

**Owner report (verbatim, three numbered items with screenshots):** shadows "cropped, sliced, hollow and incomplete … as if part of the global shadows are omitted"; "too much yellow tint during sunset and sunrise … you uniformly illuminate the whole scene in some piss very bright colour instead of naturally darkening scene and sky … the whole sky dome has same colour and luminosity … backs of the building lit with the same ugly tint instead of being in deep shadow … the sun is still too bright when it is lower than around 3-4 degrees"; "dark lines/gaps between tiles in ULTRA mode on different zoom scales, reproduces on any part of the map". He also pre-empted the cheap answer: *"if current behavior [is] due to resource or performance constraints — this is bad solution."*

**Every one was diagnosed by measurement before a line was changed**, and two of the three diagnoses killed a plausible hypothesis:

**1 — SHADOWS. `__globe.ultraLook()` at his own poses:** `viewFitM` 148,757 / 427,828 / 100,163 m against a `boundsM` of **18,000** — the ULTRA cap. Coverage **24 % / 8 % / 35 %**. Past the box three's `shadowmap_pars_fragment` returns 1.0 (lit) with a straight cut, which is the "gap" in Fuji's own shadow and the "only this spot has a shadow" frame. RC4 had already written the escape hatch: *"full visible-frustum fit stays in reserve — build only if shots after RC4 still show hard shadow edges inside the frame."* They did. **Shipped: a nested CASCADE LADDER** (`lib/globe/shadowCascade`, `ULTRA.cascades`) — and NOT `three/examples/jsm/csm`, whose rejection in `ULTRA_ARCHITECTURE` §10 stands on facts that have not changed. Each cascade is a plain `DirectionalLight` at **`intensity = 0`**: it lights nothing and only owns a depth map, and the ground's `ShadowMaterial` twins resolve through `getShadowMask()`, which **multiplies every directional shadow mask with no cascade dispatch**. §10 lists that as a reason CSM could not work here; for NESTED boxes it is precisely the mechanism — outside its box a cascade returns 1.0, so the product is the UNION and a coarse cascade can only add shadow a finer one agrees with. Zero `onBeforeCompile` surgery, so the buildings' 15-uniform injection, the ground's chained one and the ~19 raw ShaderMaterials are all untouched. Three invariants, all machine-checked: **lockstep down never up** (`WebGLLights.js:295-305,459-465` indexes `directionalShadow[]` by position among ALL directional lights then truncates to the CASTER count, so a non-caster in front of a caster silently drops the caster's shadow — `sun` is always first); **move only when refreshing** (`shadow.autoUpdate = false`, and `WebGLShadowMap.js:170` skips BEFORE `updateMatrices`, so a stale cascade keeps a matrix that still matches its map); **centred on the EYE** not pushed down the look, so the boxes strictly nest and do not move when the camera merely turns. Refresh triggers: extent · terrain epoch · eye drift > 12 % of the half-extent · key swing > 0.25° · a 1.5 s RC21-shaped staleness net. **Browser-measured after: coverage 100 % at every pose**, and cost **+3.0 ms mountain / +3.3 ms city (+7-10 %)**, VRAM **+168 MB**.

**2 — DUSK. Four mechanisms, and the load-bearing one had no name yet: the air-light had no LEVEL.** `ftwAerial` mixed toward a fixed palette stop at up to `hazeMaxK` 0.72 with no brightness term at all, so at sunset the far field became 72 % bright orange and ended up **brighter than the foreground** — the literal "uniformly illuminating the whole scene in some piss very bright colour". Alongside it: the key light **brightened** 35 % through the golden band and carried full strength to the horizon (`sunExtinctionK` dims only the DISC impostor); `EARTH.dayGradMin` 0.78 floors the slope ramp so a face pointing DIRECTLY AWAY from the sun keeps 78 % of a face pointing into it, at every hour; and the dome's horizon haze is a function of elevation above the horizon ONLY, so the anti-solar sky glowed exactly as brightly as the solar one. **Shipped `lib/globe/duskLight` + a rebuilt shared `ftwAerial` + a directional arm on the dome.** The split worth remembering: the **chromaticity** of low sunlight is PHYSICS (Kasten-Young airmass through per-channel Rayleigh+aerosol optical depth), the **level** is an AUTHORED curve — true transmittance at 0° is ~1 % of zenith and this renderer has an exposure ramp, not an adapting eye, so a physical level would black the frame out at the exact moment he wants to look at it. Measured sweep: `skyLevel` 1.000 → 0.973 → 0.806 → 0.580 → 0.282 and `directK` 1.000 → 0.959 → 0.668 → 0.166 → 0.000 across sun 26.8° → −5.4°, sun disc **0.378 at 3.4°**, key **0.002** at sunset, and a `afterglow` that PEAKS at 0.55 below the horizon (deliberately non-monotone — it is the only curve in the model that must outlive its own sky level). Two shipped values were RE-ANCHORED, not extended: `ULTRA.hemiCurve` (peaked at **1.15 at 0°** — the ambient fill was brighter at sunset than at noon) and `ULTRA.hemiTintK` **0.6 → 0.22**, because a HemisphereLight is azimuth-free and 0.6 painted the band tint onto every wall in the city at once. That is exactly "backs of the building lit with the same ugly tint"; the warmth now lives where it is directional (the reddened key, and the air-light's Mie lobe).

**3 — TILE SEAMS. Two hypotheses died first.** Killing RC25's capped mip chain (`mipLevels` 4 → 1) changed the grid **not at all**; nor did anisotropy. Suppressing the TERRAIN CASTERS removed it completely (`verify-shots/seamab-{A,B,C}`), so the caster was the seam — and it was the quantized-mesh **SKIRT**, whose length defaults to `tile.geometricError` (hundreds of metres at wide-view LODs). It was in the pipeline **twice**: it CAST (a wall on the tile edge occludes the neighbour across a band of width ≈ skirtLength·cos(elev) — parameterise a 45° sun and the skirt point δ into the band is nearer to the light than the surface behind it by exactly 2δ), and after that was clipped it still RECEIVED (a hairline remained; sweeping the caster's `polygonOffsetUnits` **2 → 1600** moved it not at all while switching the shadow pass off removed it — the apron's top edge is coincident with the neighbour's surface, reads as self-shadowed, and the `ShadowMaterial` twin paints its slate over the visible sliver). Both fixed by drawing the SURFACE CAP ONLY (`geometry.groups[0]`) for the duration of one draw — `onBeforeShadow`/`onAfterShadow` on the caster, `onBeforeRender`/`onAfterRender` on the twin. The colour pass never sees a clipped range, so the skirt keeps hiding inter-LOD cracks. Contract + unit test in `lib/globe/terrainSkirt`, and it fails SAFE: an unrecognised group layout casts and receives exactly as before.

**NEW:** `lib/globe/shadowCascade.ts` · `lib/globe/duskLight.ts` · `lib/globe/terrainSkirt.ts` · `scripts/verify-ultra-dusk.mjs` (**21/21**) · 3 test files (44 tests). **EXTENDED:** `tuning.ULTRA` (cascade ladder + 4 dusk curves + the air-light lobes + the direct/ambient split) · `GlobeCanvas` (the boot-built ladder) · `StylizedTiles` (`stepShadowCascades`, the dusk sample, the key's extinction + physical chroma, `ultraLook().cascades`/`.dusk`/`.shadowCoverM`) · `glsl.FTW_AERIAL_GLSL` · `imageryGround` · `buildingMaterial`/`buildings`/`enrichedBuildings` · `atmosphere` (the directional dome) · `sky` (the disc dims with the key it represents).

### 2026-08-27 — BEST SPOT PARKED (owner order) and the whole effort consolidated into `.claude/claude-docs/bestspot/`: the docs now say what survives, the numbers survive a wipe, and `mem:core`'s "100 PASS / 0 FAIL, go tune `graze.conf`" — the most misleading artifact in the repo — is corrected

Owner order: *"lets stop with that for now, consolidate your findings, update, merge if needed and group everything best spot (aka heatmap) relevant in `.claude/claude-docs/bestspot`… make sure we can easily continue if I will have more bugs or decide to proceed with sweep attempts."* **ENTRY POINT IS NOW `.claude/claude-docs/bestspot/README.md`.** Memory: `mem:project/wip-2026-08-27-bestspot-park`. Method: an 8-agent workflow (5 doc mappers returning structure/stillLive/supersededOrDead/citedAnchors with every claim citing a section · a shipped-state auditor · an exhaustive reference auditor with zero-result validation · a completeness critic prompted *"what will a resumer be unable to find, or be actively misled by?"*); 942k subagent tokens, 195 tool calls. **THE BUNDLE — 8 files, was 2.** `BESTSPOT_SPEC_V2.md` / `BESTSPOT_PLAN.md` / `BESTSPOT_TASTE_V1.md` MOVED by `git mv` and deliberately **NOT renamed** (76 bare-filename citations in `src/`+`test/` headers name them, and ~100 code citations point at `SPEC_V2 §x.y` anchors — renaming or renumbering breaks all of it). **THREE NEW DOCS.** **`README.md`** — index · the ONE open owner decision · **a session-label collision table** (the date letters DISAGREE across documents: "2026-08-26i" names the R-1/corridor session in `BESTSPOT_TASTE_V1.md` and the gate-refutation session in `DECISIONS.md`, so cite the CONTENT not the letter) · the diagnosis in five measured facts · the resume ladder · code entry points + dev seams + scripts · known-red · what shipped inert. **`TRAPS.md`** — every trap paid for in session time, **rescued out of `NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md`, which is gitignored (`.gitignore:3`) with NO git history and is rewritten wholesale by the next unrelated session**; four traps existed nowhere else (a window budget must drag the SHOULDERS in with it — 54.5° measured where 23° was asserted; **`finishVerify` calls `process.exit` so a bare `finally` SWALLOWS the throw**, still live in two shipped probes which also exit `9222 % 256 = 6`; the 504-optimize-dep remedy's ordering half, in particular that **curl does not warm optimizeDeps** so only a real browser load does; orchestrator state must sit ABOVE the ephemeris seam or you get a TDZ and a silent fallback), plus three that lived only in `wip-2026-08-26-bestspot-ownerbatch` (`fences.test.ts` only inspects `/^\s*(allowed|enabled):/` lines, so a new gate term is otherwise UNFENCED and nothing says so; a full-canvas pointer sweep blows the 90 s CDP cap; a verify script that only sets the temp pin proves nothing). **`MEASUREMENTS.md`** — every browser-measured number transcribed, **because `verify-shots/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:38`) and NOTHING in it can be re-derived offline**: both probe sessions' full arm tables (10 + 7 + 3 + 11 arms), the track-cost table (top 4° → K = 41 shipped · 10° → 76 · 25° → 268, the march clamping at culmination), the worldwide shipped window spans (Dnipro 16.0° · Sydney 10.5° · Reykjavík 40.5° · **Tromsø 45.5°, K = 176** · Quito/Singapore 6.1°), the ceiling arithmetic, and a **cross-run variance** section (the pick's own byte is stable to 0 across three passes; `cellsStrictlyBetter` moves 7 % and `floorFrac` 22 % — trust the former, never the latter). **BANNERS, NOT MERGES** — merging the superseded docs would destroy the narrative of how the diagnosis evolved, which is their most valuable content since each pass measured the previous one wrong; The gate is what makes the cell EXACTLY ZERO; it is not what keeps it out of the ranking — two different failures, and only the first was ever diagnosed.** **(3) THE FINDING NO PRIOR DOC CONTAINS.** Peeling `S = A_hard · A_soft^0.5 · M · G · preference` with `G = 1`: star-map 0.2176 · `+majorRoad/road:1` **0.2176 UNMOVED** (field best 0.400→0.441, so the patch fired — he is NOT on a road) · `+ladder off` 0.3235 ⇒ `A_soft^0.5` = **0.673** · `+worth.effectiveFloor:1` 0.6324 ⇒ `M_eff` = **0.512** (daylight, field-wide). `soft = 0.673² = 0.453` ⇒ exactly one rung: **`access.soft.unknown = 0.45`**. Confirmed sharply rather than left as arithmetic — **`{unknown: 1}` ALONE reproduces the whole-ladder-off value EXACTLY (0.2176 → 0.3235)** while every other class stays penalised, moving him 76th → 87.5th percentile. **His cell is charged a third of its score because the landcover raster does not know what it is** — which contradicts a rule the codebase enforces everywhere else (`notchDepthDeg = −Infinity` because "ignorance is not depth"; UNKNOWN is "a RENDER CLASS, never a low score"; the 2026-08-26g canopy withdrawal). **OWNER CALL — it re-scores every unclassified cell in every region.** **(4) THE CEILING, and it is what reorders the work:** `S_max = A_soft^0.5 · M_eff = 0.673 × 0.512 = **0.345** < 0.378` **at a PERFECT preference of 1.0** — so no gate change, no window change and no framing term can reach the top 8 while `unknown` charges 0.45. With `unknown:1` the ceiling is `0.512 · preference`; he measures 0.6324 → 0.324 against rank 4 at 0.4223 in that same arm, and `F_peak` at `F: 0.406 → ~1.0` adds `0.30 · 0.594 = 0.178` → **0.810**, within 2 % of rank 4 — the first arithmetic in this whole investigation that reaches his photograph. **(5) HIS FOUR TERMS, read directly** (whole preference weight on one term, ladder off, worth 1, gate open): `V` 0.2735 (pct 0.42) · **`L` ≥ 0.90 at percentile 1.0000 with ZERO cells better — his cell is the BEST IN THE ENTIRE FIELD on contact lowness** · `P` 0.6765 (0.86) · `F` 0.4059 (0.85, his weakest, and the term F_peak exists to move). The blend closes: `0.15·0.2735 + 0.30·0.90 + 0.25·0.6765 + 0.30·0.4059 = 0.602` vs 0.6324 measured (byte quantisation) — **the model ranks him 83rd-percentile on preference and then multiplies that by 0.673 for not knowing what he is standing on.** **SHIPPED, both byte-identical on the shipped profile:** `gates.vStarFloor` (default **0**) — `visibilityGate(v, gates, hasStar)` → `max(G(V), vStarFloor)` when starred, inert TWICE (`hasStar` defaults false at the kernel AND the leaf ships 0), `CLASS_OF` `recompose` **proven on the FUSED path and proven to move starred cells ONLY, zero starless** (the honesty claim the floor rests on); `cellScore` passes `starRay !== null`, the same `starIdx >= 0` the solver sets the flag from. And `trackWeight.topAltDeg` (default **4**) — in the PROFILE rather than on the job, a deliberate deviation from `SWEEP_MODE_MAP.md` C2 that is valid only because slice 0's `trackHash` covers the whole `trackWeight` group and rebuilds the track; `CLASS_OF` **`resweep`** (heavier than its two siblings — it decides WHICH samples exist); `opts.topAltDeg` still wins so geometry tests need no profile. Plus `TRACK_WINDOW_MAX_SPAN_DEG = 48` + `EventTrackOptions.windowMaxSpanDeg`, truncated from the NEW end decided from `uTop` vs `uBottom` (**never `upSign`/hemisphere**), applied to the azimuth bounds so the shoulders follow the budgeted edge in. **THE SUPERSET PROPERTY IS PINNED FOR THE FIRST TIME** — `set(track(top=4).azDeg) ⊆ set(track(top=10).azDeg)` on the absolute lattice, with a negative control showing the un-snapped tracks share under half — the entire hull-cache cost model rests on it. **Cost of a raise, measured at Dnipro moonrise:** top 4° → K = **41** (shipped) · 6° → 52 · 8° → 63 · **10° → 76** · 12° → 90 · 16° → 121 · 20° → 162 · 25° → 268 (the march clamps at culmination, 30° buys nothing). **FOUR TRAPS PAID:** (a) **a sample-count budget is not a span budget** — 68 lattice points becomes 3.35° at the 0.05° lattice and silently truncates the SHIPPED window, taking `bestSpotGolden`'s τ-invariance check red at 11.89 % against its 10 % bar;
