Wix cloud UNVERIFIED (prod dark behind the nameserver gate).

### 2026-08-26i — R-1 VERIFIED IN 40 MINUTES, AND IT COLLAPSED THE PLAN: the owner's cell sits in a V≈0 CORRIDOR that points at the monument to 0.23° — the alignment locus IS the shadow locus, and the gate kills it before the framing term is ever read

Owner order: *"if your concern about `the mode ships and still doesn't find your photograph` is true — maybe lets verify this as fast as possible first and i would also rather not overcomplicate architecture and implementation and maybe would just improve current mode if possible instead."* Both instincts were right. Deliverable: `BESTSPOT_TASTE_V1.md` § **ADDENDUM 2026-08-26h**; probe `scripts/probe-bestspot-r1.mjs` (NEW). Gates: **vitest 2,134/2,134** · `astro check` 0/0/6 · `npx knip` exit-0.

**THE FAST PATH WAS TO STOP SOLVING DISCS.** The planner already builds a full horizon profile at any FPV anchor (`__globe.plan().binAltDeg`, one apparent skyline elevation per azimuth bin out to 3 km). Read it ONCE at his exact cell and the moon's visibility at every instant becomes pure arithmetic against `astronomy-engine` in node — deterministic, no streaming variance, no 9 s waits. Forty minutes instead of the scheduled two hours.

**AND IT IMMEDIATELY CONTRADICTED THE DIAGNOSIS.** The planner reports the skyline toward the moon at his cell as **0.09–0.14°** — flat — giving `V = 0.9021` and `G(V) = 1.0` under the **shipped** window. The disc reports `V ≤ 0.15` and scores zero. Both cannot be true, and **the photograph is the arbiter**. Rather than pick a side, dump the field: `S ≡ V` over a 21×21 patch (63 m) centred on his pick shows **a narrow diagonal band of `V ≈ 0` running NW–SE straight through it, 15–24 m wide, with `V` rising monotonically on both sides.** Not a bad cell. Not a region. **A shadow corridor.**

**THE CORRIDOR POINTS AT THE MONUMENT TO WITHIN A QUARTER OF A DEGREE.** `way/1202608487` bears **125.7°** from his cell at **87.1 m**; the moon at his own frame is at az **125.47°** — a **0.23°** difference, less than half the moon's own diameter. The band's width is what the monument's angular extent sweeps over the window's 8° of azimuth. **He did not pick a spot the algorithm overlooked. He picked the one cell it most confidently rejects, and it rejects it FOR THE EXACT REASON HE CHOSE IT.**

**THE REFRAME, AND IT IS THE WHOLE FINDING: THE ALIGNMENT LOCUS IS THE SHADOW LOCUS.** The set of cells from which the body passes behind a landmark is exactly the set the metric scores zero. `V` asks "can you see the body"; the photographer asks "what does it pass behind". And because **`G(V)` is a MULTIPLIER**, the shadowed cell is zeroed **before the 0.30-weighted `F` — the only term that could ever say "and what it passes behind is a monument" — is allowed to run.** The information is not missing: the solver already knows the body is blocked, at which azimuths, by what (`groundSrc`), at what distance, and when it clears. **A gate throws it away.**

**WHAT THIS DOES TO 2026-08-26h's SCHEDULE — most of it is CUT, which is what the owner asked for.** The new SWEEP MODE, the per-cell argmax over T window segments, and the `TERM_BYTES_PER_CELL` solver-core refactor are all **not implied** by the evidence; the parity contract built to protect that refactor is mostly moot. **F_peak is PROMOTED from "additional proposal" to THE FIX** — the dual of `notchAt` is precisely the term that reads a shadow as a subject. The minimal change set is three items in dependency order: **(1) the `V` gate must not veto an alignment** — the single structural blocker, and nothing else matters until it moves; **(2) the window's top must reach the clearing moment**, which is a track PARAMETER (`topAltDeg` is already an `EventTrackOptions` field), not a mode and not a segment loop; **(3) `F_peak`**, whose design in `SWEEP_MODE_MAP.md` slice 2 is unaffected. **UNVERIFIED and it is the next measurement: whether 1+2+3 lift his cell into the shortlist, and what they cost every other cell. The gate exists for a reason — it is what stops a cell that genuinely cannot see the event from ranking — so softening it needs its own falsification pass, not an assumption.**

**A SEPARATE DEFECT FOUND ON THE WAY.** `__globe.plan()`'s 120-bin profile reads **0.09°** where a 20 m mass stands 87 m away at 125.7°: **the planner is missing the monument.** The disc is right and the planner is wrong — which matters because `verify-bestspot`'s D8 cross-model block is a planner-vs-disc comparison, and it must never be re-derived against the planner alone. Worth its own slice.

**PROCESS NOTE WORTH KEEPING.** The 2026-08-26f diagnosis was right about the symptom (`V ≤ 0.15` → `G(V) = 0` → `S = 0`, all measured and all still true) and **incomplete about the cause** — it concluded "his moment is outside the window" and stopped, because ten ablations all agreed and none of them could distinguish *why* `V` was low. **The thing that broke it open was a cheap disagreement between two models, followed by dumping the FIELD instead of the CELL.** A neighbourhood is legible where a scalar is not: one 21×21 ASCII patch showed in a second what ten scoring-profile ablations could not say at all.

### 2026-08-26h — THE SCHEDULE: seven sessions for sweep mode + the parity refactor + browser + Lean + docs, with the riskiest item de-risked for 2 h and the Lean answer coming back "almost nothing, and that is correct"

Owner order: *"good schedule all remaining work, sweep mode and careful and full parity to default mode refactor of solver core + verification in browser and formal verification and design docs updates for next session."* Deliverable `.claude/claude-docs/bestspot/SWEEP_MODE_SCHEDULE.md` (four independent scoping passes + a reconciler; **seven sessions, ≈ 48.5 h**), carrying an ORCHESTRATOR'S CORRECTIONS block of six MEASURED facts that override the body. Gates: **vitest 2,134/2,134 (141 files)** · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0. Memory: `mem:project/wip-2026-08-26-sweep-mode`.

**THE SCOPING FOUND A REAL DEFECT IN THE SAME DAY'S SLICE 1, AND IT IS FIXED.** `canopyUncredited` never left the solver — 5 references, all inside `bestSpotSolver.ts`, against `openSkyUncredited`'s 15 across solver + worker + feed + types. So the single biggest behavioural change of slice 1 was **unobservable in the browser**, and the 2,025 in-disc cells that flipped to UNMAPPED could not be attributed to the trees that caused them. Wired solver → worker rung → feed honesty → DEV seam in this session; the browser re-baseline is now interpretable.

**FOUR THINGS THE SCHEDULE COULD NOT KNOW, MEASURED HERE INSTEAD.** The reconciler was read-only and says so (*"I ran no gates… No browser run, by anyone"*). (1) **`verify-bestspot.mjs` is 96/101 — measured, four runs, after slice 1. Slice 1 broke NOTHING**, and the map's blast-radius prediction over-estimated: none of its three "MOVES" moved. (2) **The D8 block is not merely stale, it is RUN-TO-RUN VARIABLE** — hero rank-1 `S` read **0.3161 → 0.3338** and the failure count moved **3 → 5** across four consecutive runs, because the disc solves from what has STREAMED. A hill-climb re-derivation that optimises a noisy objective converges on noise, so **Session 4.3 must quiesce on streaming and re-measure every candidate at least twice** — which is very likely how the pin went stale in the first place. (3) **R-4 is largely de-risked**: with ~9,570 canopy stamps in the critical path, `firstInkMs` measured **43.1 ms against a ≤ 120 gate** and `refinedMs` **493.1 ms against ≤ 1,200**, so the canopy pass did not cost the latency budget and the pre-emptive memo stays cut. (4) The Dnipro disc's `coverage` moved **1.000 → 0.929** — the withdrawal working, visible, inside the shipped harness. **One correction the other way: the schedule's §0 reads an uncommitted working tree as "slices 0-1 are not merged, the brief is false".** It is not: this repo auto-ships on SessionEnd, and a dirty tree mid-session is the normal state. Its actual instruction — record the parity goldens on the commit whose parent is the landing — survives and should be followed.

**THE PARITY CONTRACT IS THE CENTRAL ARTIFACT.** Thirteen surfaces, **exact everywhere and `toBeCloseTo` acceptable on none of them** (both sides are the same code path at the same precision, so a tolerance would hide the only bug class the file exists to catch), across a seven-fixture matrix each carrying a non-vacuity assertion — including a **moonrise** fixture, because the ASCENDING arm flips `accFLow`/`accFPrev` and `lowIdx` and nothing else in the plan exercises it. **The map's proposed host for the hash was WRONG and is superseded:** `bestSpotComposition.test.ts` does not import `bestSpotSolver` at all — it tests `cellScore`, the reference the refactor may not touch — so hashing there would prove nothing about `solveTerms`/`composeScores`, which is the entire moving surface. **The two most valuable assertions in the file cannot exist at T = 1:** every accumulator in `solveTerms` is allocated fresh per call, so a missing reset between instants is **structurally invisible** until IDEMPOTENCE (T=2 same instant twice ≡ T=1) and ORDER INDEPENDENCE (T=2 reversed) exist — and they must be written BEFORE the loop. And the argmax precision question is ruled up front rather than discovered as a 1e-8 mystery: **the objective is the f32-round-tripped score**, so the winner the buffer reports is the winner the panel prints.

**THE RISKIEST ITEM IS NOT THE REFACTOR — IT IS THAT THE MODE MIGHT SHIP WITHOUT FINDING HIS PHOTOGRAPH (R-1, P 0.4, impact very high).** The owner shot at moon alt **+5.90°**, which is above `lCeilDeg = 5`, so `L = 0` **exactly** at his own instant and the 0.30-weighted lowness term is gone by construction. His ceiling under sweep is `0.15·V + 0.25·P + 0.30·F ≤ 0.70`; **if `F` does not fire at his skyline he caps at ≈0.40 — a tie with today's field best — and still does not rank.** The de-risking experiment is **2 h in node with zero production code** (Session 2.1): `topAltDeg` is already an `EventTrackOptions` field and `EventTrack` is a plain object, so `{...track, samples, windowLo, windowHi}` IS the T=2 shape by hand. Node rather than browser because the claim is STRUCTURAL (does a term evaluate to exactly 0) and node can read `fGraze` and `fGap` separately, which `__globe.bestSpotField()` structurally cannot — it publishes a byte clamped to `[displayLo, displayHi]`. Free extra arm: the owner's rank at `lCeilDeg ∈ {5, 8, 12}`, an existing recompose leaf; a zero-code answer may exist, with the honest caveat that it moves DEFAULT mode and sits inside the parity hash.

**THE CODE-LEVEL JUSTIFICATION FOR THE DEVIATION, WHICH NOBODY HAD STATED.** `alt*` is **already** a per-cell argmin over the track (`bestSpotSolver.ts:1226-1237`), but `V` and the four τ buckets accumulate over the whole `inWindow` span. **So simply raising `topAltDeg` would hand every cell a star AND average its V and dwell across a 45-minute window — literally the average the owner forbade.** The segment boundary must move `winLo`/`winHi`, not just the top altitude. Also caught: `TRACK_TOP_ALT_DEG = 4` against `lCeilDeg = 5` means **no sample in today's track can ever exceed the L ceiling**, so the dead-`L` regime is CREATED by sweep mode rather than pre-existing — which is exactly why it is unmeasured and cheap to measure.

**THE LEAN ANSWER IS "ALMOST NOTHING NEW, AND THAT IS CORRECT" — ~2.75 h, and the value is 2.5 h of TypeScript.** Six candidates run through all four conditions. FORMALIZE: `score_mem_Icc` (the five-factor product the file's own header writes out and never states — and `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Pow.Real` is already imported and **currently unused**, so the file was built for it), `max3_mem_Icc` (4 lines), `peakBound` (written AFTER the kernel exists, not before), `conf_nonneg_is_necessary` (3 lines), and the segment max as a **two-line corollary** — do not build segment machinery. **TEST-INSTEAD, explicitly:** the T = 1 parity claim (`max` over a singleton is `Finset.sup'_singleton`; the risk is allocation, indexing and ULP drift, which the exact SHA-256 covers and Lean rules out of scope — **this is the ceremony to refuse**), and "the argmax reads only rescore-or-heavier leaves" (a statement about which variables a function reads, over a lookup table — Lean needs a deep embedding, the `upperHull` mistake, 1-3 person-weeks; the right instrument is a 1 h mechanical perturbation loop). **THE HYPOTHESIS AUDIT IS THE REAL DELIVERABLE AND IT FOUND THREE UNENFORCED BOUNDS:** `0 ≤ conf` is NOT enforced (`clampResolved` is `Math.min` only) so `graze.conf.terrain: -1` persists verbatim and **inverts monotonicity — more grazing, lower score** — while `f = max(fGraze, fGap)` keeps boundedness alive, which is the exact shape that hid the weights defect for months; `0 ≤ accessSoftExponent` is NOT enforced (`clampLeaf`'s `default: return value` is fail-OPEN) so a negative exponent turns landcover into a **reward** — `wetland` outranking `path` — with the outer `clamp01` hiding it completely; and `scaleRadii > 0` has a guard pointing the **wrong way** (`if (!(scaleRadii > 0)) return 1`), re-creating the precise kernel defect GRAZE replaced, in a function with **zero direct unit tests**, while the other e-folding scale one file away IS floored. Two e-folding scales, one clamped, one not. **Plus two Lean-gate traps nobody had recorded: the audit's theorem regex is `^theorem\s+` anchored at column 0, so a `lemma`, a `private theorem` or an indented declaration is INVISIBLE to the audit and its own `audited !== theorems.length` guard cannot fire; and `npm run proofs -- --list` exits before the audit, so a green `--list` is not a green gate.**

**TEN CUTS, ARGUED.** Dropped: the two-build browser `rg8` compare (the node SHA-256 is cheaper, more precise and bisectable) · the DONE-CHECK-4 widening from 25 probes to 10 (the 21 per-field digests are the real localiser) · a dedicated F_peak browser leg (a browser leg on a disabled feature proves only that the toggle is inert) · the pre-emptive canopy memo (now measured unnecessary) · a separate docs session. Deferred with reasons: the OSM landmark layer (larger than every slice combined; the justifying fact is that `historic=memorial` steles 4 km from the owner are invisible end to end because the bake filter is `way["building"]`), re-opening `P` (it moves DEFAULT mode, which this whole design is fenced against), and the flat-field display pass (sweep will change the very distribution it exists to fix). And `peak.conf.terrain` is explicitly NOT decided in a doc — ship 1.0 mirroring `graze`, mark it unswept: *deciding an unmeasured number in a design doc is how the 37 unreachable constants got there.*

### 2026-08-26g — SWEEP MODE approved as an ADDITIVE mode and fully mapped; slices 0 + 1 SHIPPED — two silently-dead taste leaves revived, and trees finally exist (as OCCLUSION, never as subject)

Owner ruling on `BESTSPOT_TASTE_V1.md` §7: **A1 window sweep, but as an ADDITIVE MODE** — *"by default current static behavior and base absolute spot selection is fine, and just add new additional toggle to the top of best spot window"* — plus *"safely implement additional proposals for this mode (F_peak…)"*, *"fix those defects"*, and a binding TREE RULING: *"trees … are simply very crude models in this simulation and in real life are quite unpredictable and mostly simply fully obscure view, have different sizes and if not viewed from afar are not very cinematic / convenient foreground."* Mode-gating is strictly SAFER than changing the default, and it has a precedent in this very store (`ultra`) and a discipline in this very repo (the ULTRA look ships as `mix(legacy, ultra, 0.0)` — *"exactly the legacy expression"*, `tuning.ts:737`). Gates: **vitest 2,134/2,134 (141 files, +15)** · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0. Memory: `mem:project/wip-2026-08-26-bestspot-taste`. Map: `.claude/claude-docs/bestspot/SWEEP_MODE_MAP.md` (six-track parallel read + consolidator; carries the full slice plan, the byte-identical proof, the blast radius and the three cut items).

**OWNER DECISION TAKEN THIS SESSION — canopy honesty.** Presented with the measurement that only **118 of 161,823 baked tree heights (0.073 %) are integral**, i.e. ~99.93 % are a uniform random draw over a class range, the owner chose: **trees occlude, but a cell whose view is killed ONLY by modelled canopy reads UNMAPPED, never a low score.** That REVERSES the map's own §7 recommendation to defer the honesty withdrawal — and folding it into slice 1 is what keeps the field re-baseline to ONE event instead of two.

**SLICE 0 — TWO SHIPPED TASTE LEAVES WERE SILENTLY DEAD, AND NEITHER HALF OF THE FIX WORKS ALONE.** `eventTrack` bakes `trackWeight.altScaleDeg` + `.horizonCeiling` into its own per-sample weights `w_i` (`bestSpotTrack.ts:629-631, 950-957`), and `V` is integrated against `w`. But the resident track was cached on `${kind}|${localDay}` with **no profile term** (`bestSpotWorker.ts` `trackKey`), and both leaves were classed `reweigh` — which `runApply` answers from the RESIDENT TERM BUFFER, where `V` already carries the old weights, and which never rebuilds the track. So a taste pass on either leaf changed **nothing at all** until the scene crossed a local-day or kind boundary. Fixed with `trackHash` (deliberately NOT `scoringHash` — a `weights.v` tweak must stay the 0.272 ms recompose it is classed as) folded into a new exported `trackKeyOf`, plus the class promoted `reweigh` → `rescore`. **MUTATION-VERIFIED BOTH WAYS: reverting either half turns a test red, and the negative control (a recompose leaf must NOT move the key) stays green under both mutations** — without it, folding the whole hash in would pass the positive case while making every taste knob pay a full track rebuild. Nothing had gone red before because every recompose test in this repo asserts only that *some* score moved for *some* patch, never that a patch's own mechanism ran.

**SLICE 1a — `InstancedMesh.isMesh` IS TRUE, AND IT COST US EVERY TREE.** `flattenTin` traversed on `mesh.isMesh` alone, so the baked `EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing` trees fell into the TIN path and BEST SPOT flattened the **shared unit prototype at the cell's own `matrixWorld`** — one ~1 m phantom solid per cell, tagged `SRC_BUILDING`, with every real canopy missing. `scene/planFeed.ts:239-251` had the correct branch the whole time, 30 lines away in a sibling feed. **The ORDER of the fix matters as much as the branch, and this is the part a normal fix gets wrong:** `geom.boundingSphere` on an `InstancedMesh` is the PROTOTYPE's — a ~0.5 m ball at the cell root — so a branch placed AFTER the existing reject is a no-op at exactly the discs that have trees. The cull has to be PER INSTANCE. **Mutation-verified: the naive placement fails precisely the one test written for it** (`THE PER-INSTANCE CULL — a far cell whose TREES reach the disc still yields them`). Also fixed: `heightProvenance.enriched` no longer counts tree sets as surveyed roofs — read that number DOWN as the inflation being removed. No existing test could have caught any of it because `bestSpotFeed.test.ts`'s `mountSync()` mounts bare `THREE.Group`s.

**SLICE 1b — `addCanopy` HAD ZERO PRODUCTION CALLERS.** `buildDsm` tagged every solid `SRC_BUILDING` unconditionally, so `graze.conf.tree = 0.45`, `SRC_TREE` and `noteOf(SRC_TREE)` were unreachable and `SPEC_V2 §1.2`'s *"terrain, every building, bridge decks, trees"* was not what shipped — on a tree-lined avenue the model saw open sky. Canopies now fold into `canopyTop`/`canopyMask` and **never** into `solidMask`, which is not a style choice: `accessAt` reads the solid ENVELOPE through `insideSolidInterior`, so a canopy written as a solid makes every tree-lined avenue INACCESSIBLE the moment the sheet lifts to `access.aerialMinM` (5 m) — a drone "inside" a tree. **The 6 m arm of that test is the trap; the 1.7 m arm passes even in the broken version.** One shared `enuOfEcef` now carries the DSM's own height datum (`rasterizeTinGround`'s pin-5 `(e²+n²)/2R` fall-away): the PARITY test subtracts the term and demands equality, so **dropping it goes red** — 62.8 µm at 28 m, 38 mm at the collar, and exactly the "two conventions that look alike" class. `localDsm.ts:631-633` claimed the two surfaces "agree by construction" with nothing enforcing it; now something does.

**SLICE 1c — THE CANOPY WITHDRAWAL, AND ITS SECOND BOUND WAS PAID FOR IN A MEASUREMENT.** Second arm of S7's existing idiom: withhold the ray's `known` bit while still counting its weight, so `C` falls through `gates.minCoverage` and the cell reaches the UNMAPPED path that already exists — never a low score, never a cold colour (§3.1). **The rule is two-sided and each side was earned.** Upper: `outGroundSrc === SRC_TREE` ALONE would withdraw every ray that merely has trees on the skyline — including where the moon rides 10° above them — so the disc's LOWER limb must be below the canopy top. Lower: **below the eye's own horizon dip the body is set behind THE PLANET, not the tree** — a first cut without that term measured a 1.2 m hedge at 200 m driving `C = 0.412`, i.e. UNMAPPED, on a disc whose view is wide open. **Measured contribution of the dip bound: 84,899 → 39,078 withdrawals on the 5 m fixture (54 % of them spurious) while `C` moves only 0.6163 → 0.6169**, because `trackWeight.horizonCeiling` has already zeroed those samples' weight. So it does not change the verdict — **it changes the DIAGNOSIS**, and `canopyUncredited` is published precisely so a human can answer "did the trees do this?". It is therefore pinned ON THE COUNTER, with the reason written down: a counter wrong by 2× is how the wrong re-bake gets sized, and this repo paid for that once already (`droppedOutside`, 2026-08-26d). The three-case fixture is the whole claim — same mass, same place, same blocking, different PROVENANCE: the canopy ring goes UNMAPPED, **the identical ring as a BUILDING is fully credited to 6 dp**, and a 5 m ring stays MAPPED where a 22 m one does not. An earlier version of that fixture floored the canopy radius at 6 m "so it always stamps a cell", which quietly turned a 1 mm tree into a 6 m blob and made the height argument meaningless — **if a fixture has to distort the geometry to register, the GRID is too coarse.**

**THE RE-BASELINE, MEASURED IN THE BROWSER ON THE OWNER'S OWN DISC** (`p=48.45125,35.07101,477,135.1,38.0&t=1787762683150`, moonrise, via `scripts/probe-bestspot-taste.mjs`): scored **25,578 → 23,796**, unknown **8,984 → 11,009 (+2,025, i.e. 7.9 % of previously-scored cells)**, `unmappedFrac` **0.000 → 0.064**, and INACCESSIBLE **5,839 → 5,596 (−243)** — that last one is D1's phantom prototype buildings disappearing, cells that were wrongly blocked and are now standable. `rMax` is unmoved (85 → 84) and the argmax stayed in the same neighbourhood, so the change is honesty, not a re-ranking. The owner's own hand-picked cell is still `r = 0`, which is correct and expected: **its problem was never the trees, it was the moment.**

**SCOPE CALL, STATED PLAINLY: slices 2 (F_peak), 3a/3b (the sweep mode itself) and 4 (composition quotas + per-row time) are MAPPED IN FULL BUT NOT BUILT.** Landing a large `bestSpotSolver` refactor unverified would be worse than landing nothing, and slices 0-1 are independently shippable, tested and mutation-verified. **One design deviation is already decided and recorded** in the map's header: slice 3a's per-segment term buffer (T × 96 B/cell ⇒ ~163 MB at 601², which blows `bestSpotSolver.test.ts:1271`'s 105,906,176 B ceiling) is REPLACED by *T sequential solves against the resident DSM, keeping a per-cell running MAX and merging the winning instant's terms into ONE buffer* — same behaviour, today's memory, no buffer refactor, and OFF is byte-identical because T = 1 is literally today's call. The hull cache still amortises across instants via the absolute lattice's superset property. Consequence, and it is the map's own `BESTSPOT_HONESTY` rule reached by another road: **in SWEEP mode a scoring patch is a `rescore`, never a `recompose`**, because the losing instants' evidence is gone and `composeScores` cannot re-run the argmax.

### 2026-08-26f — BEST SPOT taste + coverage: the owner's missing spot scored EXACTLY ZERO, and the reason is that the disc is anchored on the geometric contact while his photograph is 47 minutes and 5.9° later — DESIGN ONLY, nothing implemented

Owner order, still mid-QA: *"notice how there are no suggested spots in the middle of the avenue, although here is my hand picked spot… how do we improve taste and coverage."* This is a DESIGN session — no `src/**` change. Deliverable `.claude/claude-docs/BESTSPOT_TASTE_V1.md`; probe `scripts/probe-bestspot-taste.mjs` (NEW, deliberately not `verify-*` so C11 does not fence it); raw numbers `verify-shots/probe-bestspot-taste.json`. Gates re-run by the orchestrator: **vitest 2,119/2,119 (141 files)** · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0. Tier: LOCAL + BROWSER. Memory: `mem:project/wip-2026-08-26-bestspot-taste`.

**THE THREE-WAY QUESTION, SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT.** His disc reproduced verbatim (`p=48.45125,35.07101,477,135.1,38.0&t=1787762683150`, MOONRISE, 3D detail on, quiesced on the finest rung). His hand-picked cell `48.451827,35.070311` is 82 m from the centre, reads **`SCORED-reachable`** — no gate excluded it — and carries display byte **0**, tied with 11,984 other cells, 13,593 strictly better, percentile 0.469. So it was neither excluded nor crowded out: **the metric scored it, and scored it zero.** Ten live scoring profiles through `__globe.bestSpotTuning` separate why. The landcover hypothesis is **REFUTED twice**: `curves.accessSoftExponent: 0` kills the whole `A_soft` ladder and his byte stays 0; `access.soft.{majorRoad,road}: 1` likewise. `lCeilDeg: 30`, `altScaleDeg: 30` (field byte-IDENTICAL to base), `depthTrustRadiusM: 200`, all three together, and all three plus both global multipliers removed — **every one leaves him at 0**. Only `gates.{vGateLo: 0, vGateHi: 0.05}` moves him (**0 → 84** against a field best of 230), and the direct read `S ≡ V` (`weights {v:1,l:0,p:0,f:0}` + gate off + headroom) measures **`V ≤ 0.15`**. `G(V) = smoothstep(0.15, 0.75, V)` is exactly 0 below `vGateLo`, so `S = 0` exactly and `shortlist` drops him at `!(scores[i] > 0)` (`bestSpotWorker.ts:834`). **H6-vs-H8 is the discriminator: H6 carried MORE score-raising patches than H8 and still read 0; the only thing H8 removed was the gate.**

**THE UPSTREAM CAUSE IS THE MOMENT, AND IT IS NOT A BUG.** `eventTrack` sweeps from airless `TRACK_TOP_ALT_DEG = 4°` down to `alt(t0) − 3ρ` (`bestSpotTrack.ts:319,323,626-627`). Independent `astronomy-engine` cross-check at his own coordinates: the disc's `contactMs` 15:57:36Z vs true moonrise 15:57:56Z — **the ephemeris agrees to 20 seconds**, so that half of the instrument is sound. But at the contact the moon is alt −0.24° / az 116.73° and **his photograph is 16:44:43Z, moon alt +5.90° / az 125.43°** — 47 minutes later, 8.7° further round, and **1.9° ABOVE THE TOP OF THE SWEPT WINDOW**. His moment is not a low-weighted sample; it is not a sample. That is also why `altScaleDeg` changed nothing: re-weighting cannot reach samples that do not exist. Two more consequences of the contact anchor, both measured in the same run: the sun is **+5.16°** at the anchor (broad daylight, `worth` near its floor, the entire field haircut) against −1.94° at his own moment, so **the instrument solved the worst-lit minute of the event**; and the scrubber read 19:44 local while the disc was a statement about 18:57 local, with nothing on screen saying so.

**"COVERAGE" IS THE WRONG DIAGNOSIS FOR THE MARKER SPREAD — REFRAMED ON EVIDENCE.** The eight markers sit on **89.8 %** of the cells scoring ≥90 % of the field's best (100 % within 60 m), and **one of them was 42 m from his own pick**. The shortlist is faithful to the field; the field is answering the wrong question. What the same run DOES expose about the eight: they span **0.3796 → 0.3989, a 5 % spread**, and **six of the eight peak at the same minute** (`leadMs` −4.1). Eight places, one moment, one composition. Separately, the field itself is barely readable: best cell **S = 0.400** against a display window running to 0.90, **46.9 %** of scored cells clipped to `displayLo`, and **87.9 %** inside the bottom two sixteenths of the window.

**WHAT THE METRIC CANNOT SEE EVEN AT THE RIGHT MOMENT.** `F_gap` is the exact DUAL of an apex composition — `notchAt` scores sky BETWEEN two masses, excludes everything within ±ρ of `az*` from both floor and shoulders (`bestSpotMetric.ts:766,786,791`) and takes `depth = min(sL,sR) − floor`, so **a lone spire at `az*` yields `F_gap = 0` exactly**; there is no term scoring mass surrounded by sky. `F_graze` is linear in the occluder's angular width then saturates (`τ ≈ c̄·q̄·conf·(W/ρ)·k`), so a 4° ridge reaches **0.9995** and a 0.3–1.0° spire apex **0.51–0.85** — out-saturated, not zeroed, and the ridge wins over a broad region while the spire is a knife-edge corridor the 25 m NMS collapses to one row. `P = ln(D/30)/ln(100)` prices a 20 m monument at 150 m at **0.35** against **1.00** for a 3 km ridge — near is exactly what a landmark shot wants. And there is **no landmark data in the pipeline at all**: the whole occluder vocabulary is `OccluderSrc = none|terrain|building|tree|deck` plus altitude, distance and a `known` bit. The Monument of Glory reaches the DSM only by luck of one tag (`way/1202608487` carries `building=yes` + `height=20` beside `historic=memorial`, and the bake filter is `way["building"]`); the `historic=memorial` steles 4 km away carry no `building` tag and are invisible to PLUX end to end.

**TWO DEFECTS FOUND ON THE WAY, REPORTED NOT FIXED.** (1) **`flattenTin` ignores `InstancedMesh.instanceMatrix`** (`scene/bestSpotFeed.ts:289-306`): it tests only `mesh.isMesh`, which `InstancedMesh` also satisfies — the sibling says so in a comment (`scene/enrichedBuildings.ts:691-693`) — so BEST SPOT flattens ONE unit-size prototype tree at each InstancedMesh's own `matrixWorld`, tagged `SRC_BUILDING`, and misses every real canopy. `scene/planFeed.ts:238-248` branches on `isInstancedMesh` correctly, 30 lines away in a sibling feed. Magnitude UNVERIFIED. (2) **`addCanopy` and `stampSolid` are dead in the shipped path** — nothing in `src/**` calls `addCanopy`, and `buildDsm` tags every solid `SRC_BUILDING` unconditionally, so `graze.conf.tree = 0.45` / `SRC_TREE` / `noteOf(SRC_TREE)` are unreachable and `SPEC_V2 §1.2`'s "terrain, every building, bridge decks, trees" is not what ships. **On a tree-lined avenue the model sees open sky.** Both cut TOWARD optimism, so neither suppressed his cell — but **§4.1 must be fixed before any taste re-calibration**, or the re-tune is calibrated against a model that thinks a tree-lined avenue is open.

**THE FOUR PROPOSALS, IN RESOLUTION ORDER (A is the enabler — B and C cannot rescue a zero).** **A. The moment becomes a dimension of the search**: sweep `T` instants across a photographic window reaching well above the horizon, keep the BEST instant per cell (never the average — an average is what makes a 90-second alignment disappear), and promote `leadMs` to the headline. The architecture was built for it: the per-ray upper convex hull is already time-invariant and measured at **0 hull builds** per within-day scrub, so a second instant costs a max-angle query + a score pass, never a hull. **Would-it-fire, checked against the captured bad case: at 16:44:43Z his own photograph proves the moon is unobstructed from his cell ⇒ V = 1 ⇒ the gate opens ⇒ the cell scores.** **B. `F_peak`, the missing dual of `notchAt`** — local skyline prominence at the body's azimuth, walking the SAME `Hg` ray array the notch already walks; plus re-opening `P` (split `P_open`/`P_subject`, or ship `depthTrustRadiusM` as a preset — 200 m lifted the field best 0.400 → 0.456 and reordered the list). Later, B2 carries an OSM landmark layer so the panel can say *"moon on the Monument of Glory, 19:44"*. **C. Diversity by COMPOSITION, not position** (cheapest item): `contact`, `bearingDeg` and `leadMs` are all already computed INSIDE the push loop AFTER selection (`bestSpotWorker.ts:913-935`) — outputs that have never been inputs; quota them from rank 2 down. Honest scope: **C would not have found his spot.** **D. Make a flat field readable** by extending the marker rule the owner already ratified on 2026-08-26e — hue field-relative, vividness absolute — to the sheet, which leaves §3.5 intact because the absolute channel is untouched.

**THE ONE OWNER DECISION (§7 of the doc):** what is the disc anchored to — **A1 window sweep (recommended)**, A2 follow the scrubber, or A3 keep the contact and only widen the window's top. A1 is the only one that answers *when AND where*, and his own hand-picked frame is the proof the answer is not at the contact.

### 2026-08-26e — BEST SPOT owner QA batch (5 items): the shortlist stops destroying itself when you click it, and the FPV preview keeps the disc still by LOCKING THE CENTRE rather than forking the aim ladder

Owner order, mid-QA of the heatmap: five immediate findings ahead of a larger batch next session. All five shipped. Gates: **vitest 2,119/2,119 (141 files, +21)** · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · **`scripts/verify-bestspot-ownerbatch.mjs` 45 PASS / 0 FAIL** (new harness; `verify-shots/ownerbatch-0826-01/02`). Memory: `mem:project/wip-2026-08-26-bestspot-ownerbatch`.

**THE STRUCTURAL ONE (item 3).** *"On click — jump into it with FPV view WITHOUT making it current spot (so again we do not recalculate full heatmap)."* Owner ruling **R2 makes FPV a CENTRE SOURCE**: `aimAnchorFor` puts the walked eye at rung 1 (`aimAnchor.ts:56-58`), so standing at a shortlisted cell re-keys the feed's T0, bumps `sourcesEpoch`, and then keeps re-solving on every step of the walk (`camGeo` mirrors past a ~0.11 m deadband; `client.cancel` makes that "one in flight", not "no work"). The repair is a **CENTRE LOCK, not a second FPV anchor**, and three facts made that the cheap one: the disc's centre is read in exactly ONE place (`stepBestSpotFeed`), so freezing it is one expression rather than a carve-out in the shared ladder that `MapWindow`, the radar fan and the focal cone all read and `aimAnchor.test.ts` pins; the frozen value is `store.centreLatDeg`, the centre the engine ACTUALLY SOLVED and echoed from the request verbatim, so `t0` is byte-identical; and the borrowed temp pin is restored on exit, so the unlock lands where it locked. **R2 is not amended** — the sheet still renders nothing in FPV, and the panel says so on its own line. MEASURED in the browser: **jobs 5 → 5 across the whole preview, `t0` and `sourcesEpoch` unchanged, pin restored verbatim, and no re-solve after ESC either.**

**Item 1 — select ≠ travel.** A row click was `setTempPin`, i.e. the centre source: it moved the disc, re-solved the field, and destroyed the very shortlist the row came from. The row body is now a SELECT (`store.selectedKey`, looked up rather than cached, so a re-solve needs no clean-up pass) and `GO →` / `◎ LOOK` / `◠ REFINE` appear on that row. The selected marker's outer halo flips `tokens.bg → accent` and it scales by `selectRadiusK 1.6` — reusing the anatomy rather than adding geometry, because the quad already reaches r = 1.00 on the axes and a ring drawn inside would cut through the rank digit.

**Item 2 — marker colour is QUALITY, and this SUPERSEDES the shipped "colour is IDENTITY, not score" rule** (`bestSpotSheet.makeMarkerMaterial`). Eight identical cyan rings carry no ranking at a glance. But re-using the sheet's own INFERNO was refuted twice over: a marker painted its own cell's colour is invisible against that cell, and the top-K lives in INFERNO's near-black foot in a real disc (a dense-Dnipro pedestrian best measured 0.381 against `displayHi` 0.9). So the markers get `HEAT_SPOTS` — lavender → ice → mint → sun-glow → sun-core, monotone in OKLab L (0.7610 → 0.9668, min adjacent ΔL 0.0292), no `tokens.accent`, no heat token, every stop brighter than any INFERNO colour a top-K cell can realistically wear. HUE carries rank, VIVIDNESS carries the ABSOLUTE display t, so §3.5 survives the renormalisation: an all-bad disc renders eight FAINT markers rather than one triumphant gold one.

**Two browser findings changed that design after it was written.** (a) Snapping each quality to the nearest of five stops gave **two colours for eight markers** — the real shortlist's `score ÷ best` spans 1.000 → 0.824 — which is most of the way back to the flat accent it replaced. Fixed with continuous interpolation, and BOTH faces interpolate in **sRGB** (`color-mix(in srgb, …)` for the swatch, a byte lerp for GL): lerping two `THREE.Color`s instead blends in the LINEAR working space and lands on a different colour than the swatch beside it. (b) Even continuous, a RATIO uses only the top fifth of the ramp, because a top-K is by definition drawn from the top of the field and the lower stops are unreachable by construction. Fixed with `shortlistQuality(score, scores)` = **(score−min)/(max−min)** over the shortlist's own span, exported from `store/bestSpot` so the panel's swatch and the GL marker share ONE formula (the panel may not import `scene/**`). Degenerate span → 1, never NaN — a NaN in a vertex attribute is a silently BLACK marker and in a swatch is `undefined.css`.

**Item 3, the other half — the hover tip.** `store.sceneHoverKey` was declared, initialised, and had **no writer anywhere in `src/`**. It does now, together with `sceneHoverScreen` (the `camera.tempPinScreen` → `.ct-pinpop` recipe), and the tip floats at the marker's own projected px saying which of the three `contact` verdicts put the cell on the list. The hit test is `bestSpotSheet.pickMarker(ndc)`, which reproduces the vertex shader's own billboard projection in PIXELS: a `THREE.Raycaster` cannot answer this question at all — every child of that group carries `raycast = () => {}`, and even without that the billboard happens IN THE SHADER, so an InstancedMesh raycast would test a plane lying flat on the tangent basis and miss by the whole tilt. It fails CLOSED on every path where nothing is drawn.

**Item 4 — the `◎ HEATMAP` chip was a readout wearing a chip's clothes.** It printed ON whenever a centre existed, could not be clicked, and the thing that actually armed the solver was "the window is open" — so opening it committed to a ~700 ms solve at whatever request was left over. Now `store.heatmapOn` is the feed's third `armed` term, `setOpen` forces it OFF in BOTH directions, and disarming takes the same path as closing (cancel, clear the mirror, release the sheet). Verified: OFF, a radius AND an event change post **zero** jobs; arming after a radius change solves the NEW radius.

**Item 5 — `REFINE THIS SPOT` did not name its target and its effect was invisible.** It acted on `topK.find(hoverKey) ?? topK[0]` — whatever the pointer last touched. It now sits on the selected row. And `refinedFromScore` (stamped in `onRefined`, the one moment both numbers exist) makes the ~1.5 s re-solve observable: the ROW prints `1 m: +0.19` / `1 m: −0.03`, and — the case the old UI could not express at all — `1 m: NO CHANGE`, thresholded at the row's own printing precision so a delta that cannot move the number beside it is never announced as one. Measured live: 1,494–2,419 ms, `−0.02`/`−0.03`.

**Two look defects the browser found and the tests could not.** A four-row marker legend pushed shortlist row #1 below the fold at the default window height — compressed to two rows plus an inline caption, because the scarcest thing on this panel is the space ABOVE the shortlist and the shortlist is the product. And `.bsp-tip` at `max-width: 21rem` ellipsised its longest line, where the clause it dropped was the RESOLUTION qualifier — 25rem, measured against the actual 57-char line.

**CARRIED, and it is NOT this batch: `verify-bestspot.mjs`'s D8 cross-model block is RED.** Confirmed by stashing the whole batch and re-running on clean master: it measures hero rank-1 **S 0.3159** where the 2026-08-24 fixture expects **0.065**, and a hero skyline of **0.97°** where it expects 40.31°. That is consistent with RC16's straddler recovery and RC17's pick-height removal landing on 2026-08-26 — both change the BUILDING geometry that reaches BOTH models. The fixture needs re-deriving; the other 96 of that harness's 101 checks are green. **`verify-bestspot.mjs`'s `openDiscAt` also had to learn `setHeatmapOn(true)`, and the call order matters** because `setOpen` forces the switch off. Housekeeping: `./tmp/rc16-chernobyl/` (9.3 MB) removed. Trap for the next harness: **Node 20 has no global `WebSocket`** — every CDP script needs node ≥ 21 on PATH.

### 2026-08-26d — THE RENDERING CHARTER IS CLOSED: RC16 shipped WITHOUT the margin bake it was sized for (its own drop counter was two populations wearing one name), and RC21 ships OFF, heartbeat-first, with the charter's predicate premise refused on evidence

**RC16 — the audit's fix was refuted by measuring the number it was named after.** The charter sized S19 as a WIDENED Overpass harvest + a margin bake + an inverse-prism crossfade ring: a 62 MB re-fetch, all 16 OSM2World converts re-run (~41 min for Dnipro alone), a new `marginM` key, and a `regions.ts` bbox change it is machine-pinned against (`regions.test.ts`). **None of it was needed.** New read-only `scripts/bake/measure-straddlers.mjs` re-runs both bakers' *inputs* to the point where each picks a grid cell and classifies the decision — IN / C_IN (centroid inside, crosses an edge) / C_OUT (centroid outside, still touches) / FAR (disjoint). **`droppedOutside` was TWO POPULATIONS WEARING ONE NAME:** 96–99.9 % of it is FAR, sitting a **median 761 m (dnipro-o2w) / 40 km (st-albans-o2w) / 36 km (chernobyl-o2w)** outside the bbox and reaching **55 km / 653 km / 149 km**, because OSM2World renders its whole relation-recursed extract — whose data bounds inflate past the sub-box, as the adapter's own comment had said for weeks without anyone connecting it to the counter. Dropping those is CORRECT. Only **123 / 61 / 1** features were real straddlers, and dropping THOSE is what left a half-building notch, because the runtime prism (`bboxClipPrismEcef`) had already erased the inside half of their Cesium twin. **The margin bake was unnecessary because the straddlers were already harvested:** `way["building"](bbox)` returns whole ways that INTERSECT the bbox and `out geom` inlines their full geometry, so there was never a data gap — only a rule that discarded the data. The same fact explains why **the extruder measures FAR = 0 at all three cities**: its unbounded clamp was safe by a property of its INPUT, not of its code. **THE RULE** now lives once, in `scripts/bake/lib/geo.mjs` (`binFeature`/`bboxIntersects`/`bboxContainsBounds`/`cellOf` — it was inline in both `main()`s with zero coverage, and the two DISAGREED across an A/B seam that `dnipro` ships both sides of): **ownership by bbox INTERSECTION, never by centroid** (the contract is "the enriched tileset draws whatever the prism removed", so anything poking into the prism is ours or it is a hole), **placement by the clamped centroid cell** (ownership guarantees the feature touches the bbox, so the clamp moves it by at most one cell — a clamp, not a teleport). **Predictions were written before the re-bake and every one hit to the unit:** dnipro-o2w 133,314 → **133,437** (+123) · st-albans-o2w 26,126 → **26,187** (+61) · chernobyl-o2w 1,706 → **1,707** (+1) · droppedFar 2,618 / 228 / 1,663; and the extruder bakes came out **byte-identical** — `diff -rq` over the whole chernobyl bake reports only `bake-manifest.json`, carrying the two new counters. Both bakers persist `straddlers` + `droppedFar` in place of `droppedOutside`. All three o2w bakes re-baked (converts were mtime-cached, so no re-convert), re-uploaded and **curl-verified LIVE on R2** at `dnipro-o2w-4` / `st-albans-o2w-3` / `chernobyl-o2w-3`; the recovered identities were then found in the LIVE sidecars — `w777741864` in chernobyl-o2w `cell-0-9`, and `w37846705` / `w1077391114` / `w122009446` / `r6443117` (a multipolygon relation) in dnipro-o2w. **RESIDUAL, MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY LEFT:** an owned feature that pokes outside is still drawn twice out there, on **212 dnipro · 229 dnipro-o2w · 96 st-albans · 111 st-albans-o2w · 1 chernobyl** (0.03–0.42 %), poking a **median 3–10 m**. That is a coincident-sliver DUPLICATE, not a hole; it is the crossfade ring's job, and **growing the prism instead punches a real hole** over a ring the bake does not cover.

**RC21 — shipped OFF, and the charter's premise refused rather than attempted.** The row asked for an "is anything animating" predicate covering uTime twinkle, every eased uniform, flights/FPV and governor DPR changes. On the 2026-08-26 recon's own numbers — **40+ independent per-frame visual-change sources across 20 files, ~14 of them asymptotic eases with NO snap** — no such predicate is both cheap and complete, and the cost of a false negative is a **frozen globe**, not RC19's stale inset. So `pipCache`'s shape is reused wholesale in `src/lib/globe/frameGate.ts`: **the heartbeat is the safety net and the epsilons are only an optimisation.** `GATE.maxStaleMs` (200 ms) bounds every miss to a low frame rate. The RC21-only addition is a **settle window**, and it is SIZED rather than chosen: the repo's own convention is to assert after ≥6.2τ, the slowest LOOK-only ease is `ULTRA.exposureTauMs` at 950 ms (haze 700, reveal 600, drape 350 all under it), so `restMs` = 6.2 × 950 ≈ 5,890 → **6,000 ms** — with camera eases (`altEaseTauMs` 2,600, `poseEaseTauMs` 1,200) deliberately excluded because they move the camera and the pose predicate already holds the clock reset for their whole duration. That sizing is **machine-checked by a unit test**, which is how the first draft caught itself reading `SKY.exposureTauMs` — a field that does not exist, yielding `NaN`. `markGateDirty()` covers tier applies, the bloom/flat-chart flip, the ULTRA chip, resize and context restore; **tile streaming and every StylizedTiles ease are covered by the heartbeat ALONE**, which is the design and is exactly why the heartbeat may not be raised casually. **Both rollbacks are unit-LOCKED rather than claimed in a comment:** `enabled: false` (the shipped default) and `maxStaleMs: 0`. `composer.render()` and the PiP blit skip TOGETHER and cannot be separated — with `preserveDrawingBuffer: false`, blitting into a skipped frame's backbuffer draws into undefined contents. **It buys GPU and power, NOT CPU:** everything above the gate, including `tilesHandle.update()`'s whole 55-step chain, still runs every frame. **The default flip is an owner call and is browser-UNVERIFIED** — it wants a full ULTRA-timelapse soak through `window.__frameGate.enabled` (whose counters are cumulative: sample twice and difference).

**Trap banked:** `bake.mjs --out` resolves against REPO_ROOT (`join(REPO_ROOT, args.out)`), so `--out /tmp/x` writes `./tmp/x` INSIDE the repo where the session-end auto-ship would commit it — `/tmp/` is now gitignored.

### 2026-08-26c — RENDERING CHARTER Group D: RC13 + RC17 SHIPPED across all five bakes, RC15 REFUTED by a measurement that needed its own negative control, and three defects nobody had a row for

**RC13 (base skirt) and RC17 (`meta.json` sidecar consumption) are DONE and browser-proven; RC15 (DSM→DTM) is REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT for buildings and re-scoped to canopy as T58; RC16 and RC21 remain.** Gates: **vitest 2,079/2,079 (140 files, +29)** · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · **NEW `scripts/verify-bake-ladder.mjs` 8/8** · `verify-rendering-charter.mjs` **85/85 ALL PASS** · `verify-chernobyl` 8/8 · `verify-bldg-override` PASS · `verify-ultra` ALL PASS · `verify-eclipse` ALL PASS. **All five building bakes re-baked, re-uploaded and curl-verified LIVE on R2** (dnipro, dnipro-o2w, st-albans-o2w, chernobyl, chernobyl-o2w) — which also **CLOSES T55** (Dnipro's shipped bakes predated the `roof:height` semantics fix).
**RC13 — the skirt costs ZERO vertices, because it is a TRANSLATION, not an extrusion.** The charter says "extrude a 3–5 m skirt below `base`"; the naive reading (append a quad per bottom boundary edge) was measured on the real OSM2World intermediates at **+59 % vertices bake-wide, +78 % on `Building` alone** — against audit S13's own acceptance clause of **"cell vert count +≤10 %"**. But the walls already HAVE a bottom rim, and lowering it is the same picture for **+0 vertices**. Receipts, verts before → after: dnipro `5,050,380 → 5,050,380` (**+0**), chernobyl `82,422 → 82,422` (**+0**), dnipro-o2w `7,714,731 → 7,729,149` (+0.19 %), st-albans-o2w `1,732,608 → 1,735,662` (+0.18 %), chernobyl-o2w `358,329 → 360,228` (+0.53 %) — and **every non-zero delta there is recovered buildings from the dedupe fix below, not skirt**. Two guards, both from measured data rather than assumption (`lib/buildings.mjs` `skirtFor`/`skirtForSoup`): a `base > 0.25 m` mass is authored to start above ground (`min_height`/`building:min_level`) and skirting it would fill a gap the mapper drew on purpose (66/127,890 Dnipro, 6/1,212 Chernobyl); and a Y-extent under 0.5 m is a flat ribbon with no wall to extend — the o2w bake runs `createTerrain=false` with no SRTM dir so its ground is the plane u = 0, and **`Cliff` (60) and `RetainingWall` (171) come back at `minY === maxY === 0`**, where a skirt would hang a 4 m curtain off a zero-height line. Skirted: 1,206/1,212 · 1,642/1,706 · 26,099/26,126 · 127,824/127,890 · 132,407/133,314. `skirtM: 4` lives in each city config; the −80 m `RESEAT_PAD_M` already covers it, so bounding volumes are unchanged and S13's "region minH still inside the ±80 m pad" holds by construction.
**RC17 — ONE schema, and the pick fence stops being a height floor.** The bakers wrote DIFFERENT shapes (`{id,osm,base,height,heightSource}` vs `{id,osm,cls}`) and both variants of the same city ship side by side, so a runtime consuming either would have behaved differently across the A/B seam. Unified in NEW `scripts/bake/lib/meta.mjs` (schema **2**): `{id, osm, cls, base, top, skirt, src}` under a `{schema, variant, skirtM}` header, with **`base`/`top` MEASURED from the emitted vertices in both bakers** — the extruder's `params.height` is the EAVE with the roof above it and the adapter has no tags at all, so measuring is the only definition that means the same thing on both sides. Runtime: NEW pure `src/lib/globe/enrichedMeta.ts` + consumption in `scene/enrichedBuildings.ts`. **The defect this removes was live, not hypothetical**: `ENRICHED.overrideMinPickHeightM = 2.5` was a geometric proxy for a semantic question and failed in BOTH directions — a single-storey outbuilding was unpickable, while every street lamp, flagpole and 30 m transmission pylon cleared it and was fully pickable AND **rescalable**. The tuning comment claimed "~4.5 % of features"; the shipped Chernobyl o2w bake is **30.6 % non-building (516 of 1,688), 273 of them `HighVoltagePowerTower`** — wrong by ~7×. Browser-measured over one central-Dnipro view: **89 non-building features RECLAIMED** from the old floor. The constant STAYS as the documented fallback for a bake with no sidecar; only the hack is gone.
**The ordering fix is the part worth remembering.** The sidecar tells `load-model` a feature's class AND its true base — and load-model is where the pristine per-run capture happens and where persisted U8 overrides are re-applied. A sidecar landing afterwards would mean the first pick on a fresh cell silently used the old floor, and a re-applied override would begin easing about the SKIRTED base and then have the pivot move underneath it mid-ease. Fixed by making the existing `FTW_ENRICHED_FORCE_CACHE` plugin **resolve the MODEL's own fetch behind the sidecar** (`Promise.all([glb, primeMeta(u)]).then(([r]) => r)`) — the race is gone at no cost, since the two run concurrently and the sidecar is kilobytes against the cell's megabytes. So `f.baseY` is now the building's TRUE base (geometric min + `skirt`), which is what all five of its consumers already meant by "base": the U8 scale pivot, the ghost rebase, `growBoundsFor`, `bakedHeightM` and `buildingTopWorld`.
**THREE DEFECTS NO CHARTER ROW NAMED, all fixed here.** (1) **The o2w cross-sub-box dedupe keyed on the glTF NODE NAME — and OSM2World names a node after the OSM `name` TAG when the element has one** ("Building Sainsbury's", "Building Теплиця"), so the set collided on genuinely different buildings and dropped them with no signal. Re-keyed on `` `${cls}|${extras.osmId}` `` (present on 100 % of named mesh nodes, already read four lines later for the sidecar; the class stays in the key so a `Building` and a `BuildingPart` on one element cannot cannibalise each other). **RECOVERED: +231 Buildings in dnipro-o2w, +24 in st-albans-o2w, +18 in chernobyl-o2w**; deduped counts fell 20,521→20,281 · 423→399 · 1,367→1,319. (2) **A re-bake could never have reached a returning browser**: the R2 worker serves `.glb` as `max-age=31536000, immutable` and re-bakes REUSE FILENAMES, so `buildTileset` now stamps `?v=<tilesetVersion>` on every content uri — safe because content dispatch reads the glTF MAGIC BYTES and only falls back to the extension (`TilesRenderer.parseTile:670`, `case 'gltf': case 'glb':`), and it propagates within the 5 min `tileset.json` is cached. **The runtime strips it back off** through `cellUriOf`: that string is the persistence key for U8 override rows and banked cell seats, so leaving the version in would drop every saved edit on a version bump alone. The SIDECAR url keeps it (twin lifetimes). All six `tilesetVersion`s bumped. (3) **`droppedOutside`/`droppedPolygon` were logged but never persisted**, so no baseline existed to regress a straddler-rule change against — now in the o2w manifest, which is exactly what RC16 will need.
**The cache-buster's own trap, and it bit twice:** `endsWith(".glb")` stops matching a `?v=`-suffixed URL and fails **silently**. It would have dropped both the force-cache claim and the sidecar prime in `enrichedBuildings.ts` (fixed pre-emptively to `/\.glb(\?|$)/`), and it DID break `verify-chernobyl.mjs`, which reported "the o2w tileset loaded but streamed ZERO cell glbs" against a page streaming them perfectly — four sites patched there.
**M11 TAKEN, and RC15's building half is REFUTED.** NEW read-only `scripts/bake/terrain/measure-dsm-signature.mjs` (writes nothing, downloads nothing uncached): per footprint, the DSM over its own pixels minus the median of a local bare-earth annulus excluding **every** footprint (a neighbour's roof is not bare earth), water and canopy — **with a NEGATIVE CONTROL running the identical statistic on random NON-building pixels, without which the number cannot be read at all**, because a pixel-vs-neighbourhood statistic has a spread on any terrain that is not a plane. Dnipro: footprints median **0.39 m** vs control **0.05 m** (separable **+0.34 m**); p95 **5.78 vs 5.60**; share > 5 m **6.4 % vs 6.2 %**. Chernobyl: median **0.45 vs −0.04** (**+0.49 m**); p95 **6.17 vs 5.88**. **The tail is entirely control-dominated** — the "6.4 % of footprints with a >5 m building signature" is matched by 6.2 % of random ground. Mechanism: **95.7 % of Dnipro's footprints (122,398/127,878) cover exactly ONE source pixel** (83.4 % at Chernobyl); at a 20.5 × 30.9 m posting a median ~200 m² building is a third of a pixel, so the sample is an area-weighted blend dominated by street and yard and the roof contributes ~4 % of its height (median ratio to own height **0.05**). Only 139 footprints (0.1 %) cover 17+ px, and those do register (median 6.4 m). **So punching + inpainting footprints out of a 30 m DSM would move the served mesh ~0.34 m at the median — below the noise floor of the statistic used to detect it, ~1 % of the source posting, far under L13's ~38 × 25 m served posting, and dwarfed by both M5's 14.20 m within-cell relief and RC13's 4 m skirt. RC15 as chartered should NOT be built** (same family as the RC12 and RC28 refutations). **But the CANOPY half is real and is a different slice** — audit gap #11 names it in the same breath, and it is mechanically unlike buildings because a wood covers hundreds of CONTIGUOUS postings and therefore fully occupies the sample: contiguous (17+ px) wood/park polygons measure **+1.01 m median at Dnipro (n 365, p95 10.36)** and **+3.78 m at Chernobyl (n 77, p75 7.63, p95 14.37)**, four decades of unmanaged Polesian regrowth — and Chernobyl is exactly where the bake plants **166,599 trees ON TOP of a surface that already IS the canopy**. → **T58**, which compounds with T57.
**Traps banked:** `__globe.debugSeats` does not exist, the DEV seam is **`__globe.enrichedSeats()`** · `verify-rendering-charter`'s **RC11 leg was sampling a cumulative-since-page-load counter once** and read 9.8 % where the same engine measures 47 % at 10 s and 87 % at 120 s — now polled, and a memo that genuinely never re-asks still fails, so the assertion did not weaken · **the 504-Outdated-Optimize-Dep trap has an ordering half nobody wrote down**: moving `.vite/deps` aside and THEN starting `wix dev` is not enough, because the re-optimisation happens during the first warm load and invalidates the modules that load already fetched (`__globe` never appears) — bring the server DOWN again after that first warm load, restart, warm once more.
