### 2026-08-26b — REGION #4 Chernobyl / Pripyat: two buildings bakes + a GLO-30 patch, LIVE on R2 — and the bake found three pre-existing defects, the loudest of which was rendering the New Safe Confinement at double its height

**Owner ask (explicitly scoped away from the remaining RENDERING CHARTER work — Group D and RC21 are untouched):** a high-fidelity OSM buildings bake plus a Copernicus height bake over a ~10×10 km patch capturing *both* Pripyat city and the Chernobyl reactor, full pipeline, uploaded to R2. **Gates: vitest 2,050/2,050 (139 files, +5) · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · `verify-chernobyl.mjs` 8/8 in headless CDP Chrome.** Dnipro / St Albans / Everest bakes, caches, prefixes and the default runtime path untouched throughout.

**The box.** `[30.006, 51.352, 30.15, 51.442]` = 10.01 × 10.02 km on 51.3973/30.0780, the midpoint of Pripyat centre and ChNPP unit 4 — they are only 3.45 km apart, so one box holds both easily. grid 10 → ~1 km cells. Deliberately outside: the *town* of Chernobyl (~15 km SSE) and the Duga-1 radar (~10 km S), both of which need a taller box than 10×10. **Runtime cost of the whole region: ONE entry in `src/lib/globe/regions.ts`** — no env edit, no `tuning.ts` edit, because `resolveEnrichedSelection` already routes a `#p=` pose into its containing region and segment-swaps the env URL, and `StylizedTiles` already reads `r.terrain`.

**Shipped:** `cities/chernobyl.json` + `cities/chernobyl-o2w.json` (subGrid 2) · the `chernobyl` EGM2008 grid in `terrain/geoid.mjs` (`[30,51,31,52]` @ 0.25°; bilinear reproduces `GeoidEval` at the probe point to 3.9 mm) · the `regions.ts` entry · `scripts/verify-chernobyl.mjs` (8 checks; the worked template for region #5). **Numbers:** classic 1,212 buildings / 72 tiles / 9.20 MB · o2w 1,688 features (1,172 Building, 379 pylons) / 74 tiles / 358k verts / 16.94 MB · **166,599 trees** · terrain 1,488 files / 3.6 MB, probes **bias 0.3 / 0.0 m, spread 2.3 / 2.0 m, rim seam 0.5 m** — all on the DEFAULT tolerances, which is why the config deliberately omits `probeBiasTolM`/`probeSpreadTolM`. Config values were tuned against a *measurement* of the box rather than copied from Dnipro: 721/1,275 footprints (56.5 %) already carry `height` or `building:levels` — the best-mapped box in the repo, hand-traced by the Pripyat mapping community — and of the 554 that don't, 457 are `building=yes` with a **median footprint of 200 m²**, hence `defaultHeightM 6` against Dnipro's 8.

**The C6 call, made narrowly and flagged rather than buried.** The built-in blocklist matches 63 footprints here. `exclusion.tags` replaces it with the same list minus **exactly one rule, `["power","generator"]`**: 54 stay excluded (45 `power=substation` — all `substation=minor_distribution` neighbourhood ТП kiosks — and 9 abandoned `military=*` checkpoints/barracks), and the 9 returned are reactor blocks 1, 2, 3, 6 plus one diesel house. C6 protects *live* Ukrainian military and critical-energy infrastructure; every object returned is a permanently shut-down RBMK block inside a licensed-tourism exclusion zone, and OSM itself tags three of the four `disused:power=generator` **with their shutdown dates** (1996-11-30 / 1991-10-11 / 2000-12-15). The structures that actually read as "Chernobyl" from the air needed no override at all — the New Safe Confinement, the sarcophagus, reactor 4's ruins and the 800 m turbine hall carry no `power=*` tag and were never excluded. What made the override worth making rather than shrugging at is that **reactor 3 shares a wall with reactor 4**, so leaving it out punched a visible hole beside the arch. **Reverting is deleting the `tags` key.** The override reaches the o2w bake too (same merged config at `bake-osm2world.mjs:126`) — all 8 landmark OSM ids verified present in both bakes' sidecars.

**Three pre-existing defects, all found by baking, all fixed.** (1) **`roof:height` was ADDED to a tagged `height` instead of subtracted from it** (`lib/buildings.mjs`). OSM's `height` is the TOTAL including the roof; `building:levels` counts storeys BELOW it — the extruder treated both as the eave, so every co-tagged building rendered taller by exactly `roof:height`. The degenerate case is the NSC (`height=110 roof:height=110 roof:shape=round→gabled`), which came out **a 220 m ridge**. The fix subtracts only when `heightSource === "height"`, caps `roof:height` at `total − 1` so the tagged total is preserved *exactly*, and **moves the `min_height` clamp below the roof block** — clamping the base against the pre-roof total could seat it above its own wall top and invert every quad. +5 regression tests; blast radius measured over every cached Overpass response (164/128,649 Dnipro — all small — - 0/25,510 St Albans, 1/1,275 here) → **T55**, Dnipro not re-baked. (2) **The rim blend died on `ENFILE`** — the *system-wide* file table, squeezed by the IDE's own `lake serve`/`lean --server` holding ~16k fds. The blend is ~1,000 serial CWT fetches and is **not idempotent**, so a mid-way crash means a full re-bake: added `withFdRetry` and, more importantly, **narrowed both `.catch(() => null)`s to ENOENT**, because an ENFILE swallowed there silently left a rim tile UNBLENDED — a height step at the seam nothing downstream would flag. (3) **`upload-r2.mjs --terrain` gated on `layer.json`, which mago writes BEFORE the blend, prune, availability post and probe** — so the interrupted tree from (2) would have uploaded clean-looking, unblended and unverified. It now also requires **`patch-info.json`**, the last thing `bake-terrain.mjs` writes and the only honest completion marker (verified by pointing it at the broken tree first). Plus a fourth, smaller: `bake-terrain.mjs`'s *extent* probe assumed `extentMaxDepth ≤ bakedMax` while the city probe already clamped, so it printed its "topped out at L12" note, passed containment, then died on a raw ENOENT for a level never written.

**L12, not L13 — and the ceiling is the tool's heuristic, not the data.** Copernicus GLO-30 coarsens LONGITUDE sampling above 50° N to hold ~30 m on the ground as meridians converge, so `N51_E030` is **2400×3600 px (1.5″ lon × 1.0″ lat)** where dnipro's `N48_E035` and everest's `N27_E086` are both 3600×3600 — measured off the cached COGs, not inferred. **mago clamps `-max` on pixels-per-degree, not ground metres**, so the same ~30 m source lands one level shallower: asked 13, got 12. In ground terms this box is 29.2 × 30.9 m (nearer square than Dnipro's 20.7 × 30.9) while L12 posts ~48 × 54 m, so about a third of the source's detail goes unused. Not worth fighting here — 29 m of relief across the probe tile, 2.3 m measured spread — and the only route to L13 is resampling the COG in Node, which the 2026-08-18 ruling forbids outright. → **T56**.

**Browser-verified 8/8** (headless :9333; the owner's persistent :9222 Chrome was down): region resolves to `chernobyl-o2w` and *not* the dnipro registry head · 14 o2w cell glbs, 0 errors · 32 terrain tiles at levels 9-12, max L12 as configured · heights 131.2..174.3 m over 5 probes · NSC cell classic **110 m (was 220)** / o2w 125 m · `?enriched=chernobyl` → 12 classic glbs and ZERO o2w · `?enriched=off` → nothing · Pripyat centre → 25 o2w glbs. The shots show the NSC as a real barrel dome (the o2w tier's payoff — the extruder's `round`→OBB-ridge cannot do it) and Pripyat's slabs with forest closed through the city. **Two probe traps cost most of the verify time, both false negatives on a working page:** `performance.getEntriesByType("resource")` **caps at 250 entries** while one settled globe frame issues ~1,700 requests (1,517 ArcGIS alone), so it reported an empty resource list — use the CDP `Network.responseReceived` log; and the terrain patch is claimed by the ground renderer's `fetchData` hook only *after* the camera settles over the region, so a fixed settle read zero tiles on a page that went on to stream 32 — poll, don't sample once. (Also `Network.setCacheDisabled`: a memory-cache hit fires `requestServedFromCache`, not `responseReceived`.) And one of mine: traversing `__globe.enriched.group` comparing `geometry.boundingBox.max.y` mixes LOCAL spaces under different parent transforms — the 152.7 m it reported was not the height of anything; replaced with the baked-glb accessor max, which is the shared ENU frame the bug actually moved.

**R2 LIVE, curl-verified through the Worker:** all three prefixes 200 + `access-control-allow-origin: *` + correct content types (`.glb` → `model/gltf-binary`, `glTF` magic confirmed by ranged GET; `.terrain` → `application/vnd.quantized-mesh`); `enriched/dnipro/` and `terrain/dnipro/` still 200. 1,785 files / 28.6 MB across `enriched/chernobyl/`, `enriched/chernobyl-o2w/`, `terrain/chernobyl/`; bucket ≈ 430 MB of the 10 GB free tier. **Dev streams the LOCAL bakes by design**, so R2 is curl-verified rather than browser-verified — an intentional split, not a gap. Incidentally **this is the first bake to ship `.meta.json` sidecars** (72 + 74, uploaded): the writers already existed in both bakers and the other three bakes merely predate them, so RC17's writer half arrived for free — its runtime-consumption half is still open. New backlog: **T55** (Dnipro re-bake owed to the roof:height fix), **T56** (the L12 ceiling), **T57** (Pripyat's unmapped self-seeded street trees — the canopy-raster tier, whose recorded blocker "needs a GeoTIFF reader" is now stale, since `geotiff` is already a dependency of the terrain bake).

### 2026-08-26 — RENDERING CHARTER Group E + RC25 + RC30: four slices shipped, and the two that mattered most were both fixed by a MEASUREMENT that contradicted the design note it came from

**Shipped: RC18, RC19, RC20, RC25, RC30.** Gates: vitest **2,045/2,045 (139 files, +2)** · `astro check` 0 err / 0 warn / 6 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · **`verify-rendering-charter.mjs` 85/85 ALL PASS**.

**RC18 — the governor lever split.** `applyTier` became `applyTierRenderer` (DPR → composer realloc, bloom, the AO gate, `tierOverlayPx`, the two DPR mirrors) + `applyTierTiles` (error targets, LRU cap/floor, queue caps, foveation, street/lattice budgets), with a pure `planTierApply(next, appliedTiles, fpvActive, livePromote)` in `lib/globe/quality.ts` deciding which half lands. A governor **PROMOTE inside FPV now lands its tile half immediately**; the renderer half still parks. A DEMOTE never splits — shrinking a cap mid-FPV evicts everything outside the visible set and then discards each fresh parse against the full cache, which is the U2/A9 loop. The safety argument is not prose: `quality.test.ts` asserts the tier TABLE is monotone (a promote only ever lowers an error target and raises a cap), so if a future tier edit breaks that, the split fails a unit test rather than becoming an eviction storm. **Browser-proven at Dnipro FPV: tileTier=high landed while tier stayed low, pending=high, DPR unchanged, ZERO overlay rebuilds.** The one hazard the split opens — promote-then-demote inside one FPV leg — is closed structurally: `planTierApply` returns BOTH halves for every non-FPV call including equal tiers, so FPV exit always re-converges. Rollback knob `QUALITY.leverSplit.livePromoteInFpv`. Also: the lean-shell flat-chart flip now re-applies the RENDERER half only, instead of re-writing every tile lever on every 2D↔FPV leg.

**RC20 — the T34 ground-LRU rest-trim, and the measurement that re-scoped it.** The defect is one line of `LRUCache.js` 0.4.28: `hasBytesToUnload = unused && cachedBytes > minBytesSize || …`. A single tile the traversal did not visit, plus a cache above the **FLOOR**, starts an eviction — the cap is never consulted. A mode flip marks the whole previous working set unused in one frame. Fix = a bounded **flip bank** (`lruBankFloorBytes` + `bankWindowMsLeft`, `QUALITY.lruBank`): hold the floor near the cap for `holdMs` after each FPV boundary crossing. Keyed on `fpvActive` and NOT `flatGround` — the flat latch has an un-hysteresed altitude term that would re-arm the bank every frame for a camera parked at 120 km, silently converting a bounded bank into a permanent raised floor. **M13 IS ANSWERED, and it says the desktop half of T34 does not exist:** on `high` the ground cache rests at **109.8 MB against a 322.1 MB floor** — the trim condition never arises. The bank ships enabled for mid/low only; the desktop leg proves the off-state (the library's non-integer 0.3/0.4 GiB pair, literally untouched). A sweep over every reachable cap also found that the shipped `lruFloorBytesForCap` INVERTS at cap 1–2 (`round(1.5) === 2`); unreachable in production, pinned in a test rather than "fixed", because its exact arithmetic is the byte-identical restore path.

**RC19 — the /m PiP, and a banked design note that was WRONG.** The second full scene render is now cached into a `WebGLRenderTarget` and blitted with a `FullScreenQuad`; the blit runs every frame (that is the anti-flicker half — `composer.render()` overwrites the rect, so a skipped paint alternates between the miniature and the full-scale view), and only the scene render is cached, on a camera/projection/sun delta with a `maxStaleMs` heartbeat. **The banked note said the blit material needs `toneMapped: false`. Source says the opposite:** three forces `NoToneMapping` on anything rendered into a render target (`WebGLPrograms.js:178-186`) and encodes into a target in the working colour space, so the target holds raw linear HDR and the blit is the ONLY place `NeutralToneMapping` can still be applied — `toneMapped` must stay TRUE. Shipping the note as written would have shipped an un-tone-mapped, clipping miniature. Also: `pipQuad.dispose()` is never called — `FullScreenQuad.dispose()` disposes three's MODULE-LEVEL shared triangle that bloom, output and GTAO all draw with. The sun is compared as a DIRECTION so ULTRA's `lightDistM` swap is not read as a view change. `PIP.maxStaleMs: 0` is the rollback and is unit-locked as byte-identical to the pre-RC19 pass.

**RC25 — the capped mip chain, and the 89× measurement that rewrote it.** Four charter premises died. (1) The crux — "an incomplete chain renders black unless `TEXTURE_MAX_LEVEL` is set" — **does not apply**: three never sets that parameter anywhere, and with `generateMipmaps` false it allocates IMMUTABLE storage sized to `mipmaps.length`, which is complete over exactly its allocated levels. The black-chain failure belongs to the mutable WebGL1 path this renderer never takes. (2) The artefact is not a colour bleed but a **double alpha multiply** (the composite is non-premultiplied, the drape shader premultiplies at sample time). (3) The dominant seam is neither — adjacent composites share NO border texels, so coarse levels average disjoint sets across a shared edge; that is a C0 discontinuity no filter removes and only the CAP bounds. (4) "3–4 levels" and "≤ +33 %" cannot both hold: 4 total levels are +32.81 %, five are +33.20 %. **The implementation was then rewritten by a measurement.** The first version read each composite back with `getImageData` and filtered in JS: **5.36 ms per composite on the main thread** (4.10 readback + 1.26 filter), with ~450 composites per flight — it stalled the page so hard that CDP `Runtime.evaluate` stopped returning and the harness sat silent for fifty minutes. Halving with canvas `drawImage` instead: **0.06 ms, 89× cheaper** — and it is the CORRECT filter, because canvas 2D composites in premultiplied alpha, which is the exact inverse of the shader's multiply. **Browser-proven: 357/357 composites chained at exactly 4 levels, VRAM ×1.328125 = 85/64 to the digit, and the off-state proven by RETURNING to it (mipMax 0, bytes === level-0 bytes).** The LRU is re-billed: `MemoryUtils.getTextureByteLength` scales by 4/3 only for AUTO mipmaps, so an unbilled manual chain would park ~200 MB past ULTRA's 600 MB ground cap — the U2/A9 loop reached from the accounting side. All three LRU writers now go through one `applyLruBand`.

**A user-visible consequence, measured and now documented:** creation-time levers (anisotropy, the mip chain) reach almost NOTHING on a mid-session flip. The ground cache never turns over — RC9 already measured that the whole Dnipro drape fits inside the desktop cap, and squeezing the cap does not help because the tiles under the camera are in the renderer's `usedSet`. Flipping the chip mid-session left **2 of 321** composites chained; with the chip on at BOOT, **452 of 452**. These levers are delivered by a RELOAD — the same state RC26 already surfaces on the ULT chip.

**Harness lessons, both paid for in this session.** A probe that reads a field which does not exist **fails OPEN**: the RC18 leg read `u2().fpv?.active` (it is `__globe.fpv()`, a function), got `undefined`, and reported "not in FPV" while the engine was demonstrably in FPV with the split already landed. And every CDP call now carries a 90 s timeout — an unbounded `Runtime.evaluate` turned a page stall into a fifty-minute silent hang with zero output.

**RC30 shipped**: `rendering/RENDERING_ARCHITECTURE.md` — the as-built doc, absorbing the audit §2 tables and ULTRA §8/§10, with the five refutations (M5 curvature 4.0 %, M7 zero parent wins over 47k samples, RC28 depth, audit S10 refinement, and now M13/T34 desktop) stated as measurements.

**NOT done, and why.** **RC21 (on-demand render) was not built.** Read-only recon mapped 40+ independent per-frame visual-change sources across 20 files, ~14 of them asymptotic eases with no snap, and its verdict was that the live default is not safely shippable — a false negative is a frozen globe. That judgement is on record with the full inventory; the heartbeat-bounded pattern RC19 just shipped is the shape RC21 should take. **Group D (RC13/RC15/RC16/RC17) was not started** — bake infrastructure was confirmed available this session (R2 credentials, Java 21, the OSM2World jar, 1.7 GB of Overpass caches, the existing bakes), so it is unblocked, not blocked.

- **2026-08-25e · RENDERING CHARTER, GROUP F + RC29 + RC22 — the two ULTRA seams closed, the chip's hidden state surfaced, and the memo's own counter caught an undersized constant.** Gates: **vitest 1,996/1,996 (137 files)** · astro check 0 err / 0 warn / 5 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · `verify-ultra.mjs` 29/29 · `verify-eclipse.mjs` 38/38 · **`verify-rendering-charter.mjs` 70/70 ALL PASS** (+14 checks; shots `charter-09..10`). Tier: LOCAL + BROWSER. · **RC23 — the ULTRA × ECLIPSE seam.** Every ULTRA look term is driven by SOLAR ELEVATION, and an eclipse does not move the sun: at totality the band curve still said "day", so the aerial perspective went on painting a day-tinted veil over a world the eclipse had just darkened and the hemisphere fill stayed warm. The fix is three multiplies rather than a new curve, and the principle is the reason why — **under an eclipse ULTRA's day-driven additions fade toward BASELINE**, because the darkening already lives in the shaders baseline carries (`uFtwEclipse` on the ground, `uEclipse` on the dome, `× eclipseK` on the key). Haze target and the hemisphere TINT MIX both scale by `eclipseK`; exposure deliberately does not (it is a taste lever, owner A/B AB5). Browser-measured at the owner's Burgos totality: ground haze **0.0253** at `eclipseK` 0.040, against a golden-hour value of 0.1138. · **RC24 — the golden-hour dome seam, the one visible SEAM the ULTRA track shipped with** (`ULTRA_ARCHITECTURE` §12). The dome tints its horizon haze from GOLDEN's bell over solar elevation; ULTRA's ground tints its aerial perspective from a four-stop band curve spanning 36° — two different curves meeting at the terrain/sky junction, which is exactly where the eye is at dusk. The coupling is deliberately NOT a second curve in `atmosphere.ts`: the orchestrator pushes the ground's **own effective, already-gated, already-eased** haze value and the band tint it is using, so the dome moves when the ground moves, through the ground's altitude / flat-chart / dark-drape gates, and cannot drift onto its own schedule — the same structural trick `FTW_AERIAL_GLSL` uses to keep ground and buildings together (share the emitted value, not the intent). Only the HAZE band is pulled, not the zenith: the haze is the band that meets the terrain, and pulling the zenith would fight the `skyBudget` guard for no visible gain. `ULTRA.domeTintK 0.45`, below `hemiTintK` on purpose — the dome is the background the golden band is read against. Browser-proven: dome pull **exactly** `groundHaze × 0.45` and the dome and ground carrying the IDENTICAL band tint hex. · **BOTH OFF-STATES ARE EXACT, and that is the load-bearing half**: `mix(x, y, 0.0)` is `x`, and the run asserts `=== 0` (not `< ε`) with the chip off at totality, then flips the chip on, then off again and re-asserts EXACTLY 0 after the snap (>6.2τ on `exposureTauMs` 950 — a wait, not a round number). · **RC26 — the chip's hidden state, surfaced.** Three of ULTRA's shadow levers are CONSTRUCTION-TIME (three latches a shadow map's size on first render and recompiles every material on a live `shadowMap.enabled` flip), so a mid-session toggle moves every eased lever and leaves the shadow rig on the boot profile. Nothing said so — the chip lit up and the rig quietly did not, which reads as a weak feature rather than as a pending reload. NEW `ultraBootSnapshot()` freezes the boot answer (memoized, because the pref moves the instant the user clicks and a later read would answer "what is it now", not "what did we build with"; the ordering is safe by construction since the chip lives in the panel whose first render necessarily precedes any toggle of it). `pref !== snapshot` drives a hairline warn dot on the chip plus a tooltip that says what to do. GlobeCanvas now reads the same snapshot, so UI and rig cannot disagree. The ULTRA flag fence (`fences.test.ts:212-308`) still passes — the new function names neither the flag nor a new owner file. · **RC27 — two design notes landed where they will actually be read.** The `ULTRA.shadowMapSize` 8192² rollback criteria (three named triggers, and the measurement that decides it — `metresPerTexel` against the live `boundsM`, which RC4 made variable). And the one that would have bitten silently: **if `AO.enabled` is ever flipped on, AO becomes an ULTRA lever by accident** — `updateAoEnabled()` gates on `tier === "high"` and the ULTRA chip PINS the tier to `high`, so on a machine the governor had stepped down to `mid`, turning ULTRA on would also turn AO on. A coupling nobody chose, in no tunable, inside a track whose whole contract is "the chip off changes no pixel". Two honest options recorded; the instruction is not to leave it implicit. · **RC29 — the two convention lines the audit scheduled and nobody wrote.** The `GlobeControls` radians-into-a-degrees-function bug (`Ellipsoid.js:361` emits radians, `:523` reads degrees ⇒ the far plane is 169.4 m / 0.094 % short at Dnipro), latent only because `MIN_ELEVATION = 2550` overshoots the FPV horizon ~39× — **with the reason not to fix it locally: eight modules scale their impostor anchors off `camera.far`.** And the vertical-datum asymmetry: the BAKE is geoid-corrected (`geoid.mjs`'s checked-in EGM2008 grids), EXIF deliberately is not (D4 binding; the `Math.max(sliderAlt, terrainH + 1.7)` clamp is the sanctioned mitigation), **do not add a runtime geoid** — plus the trap that paid for the line, the clamping lookup that answered Everest with Dnipro's corner and turned a correct bake into a 48 m phantom fault. · **RC22 — A/B-PREP only, exactly as chartered: three mobile knob proposals recorded in `tuning.ts` with their rationale and ZERO behaviour change**, because each is a real-device judgement under T1 (lean `overlayResolution2dPx` 512→384, a NEW lean-only `shadowMaxAltM` 8 km against the shared 30 km, and `queueCaps.low.download` 8→6 with parse left alone since it is main-thread glb decode). · **THE COUNTER EARNED ITS KEEP: `GROUND.heightMemoCapacity` 20_000 → 100_000.** RC11's memo overflowed, and the `overflows` counter added alongside it is the only reason that surfaced rather than reading as unexplained frame cost — the constant was guessed from "a few thousand buildings" and the browser measured the real working set at **39,302 buildings + 60,527 tree instances across 101 loaded cells at Dnipro alone**, with the memo at 18,457 live entries. Cost at the new cap ~15 MB against a ground LRU in the hundreds. · **HARNESS TRAP, RE-PAID:** the first three runs of this session died with every island failing to hydrate. Not the code — `504 Outdated Optimize Dep` on the vite dep graph (`curl` on the dep URL is the diagnostic; the page itself answers 200 the whole time). Moving `node_modules/.vite` aside is not enough on its own: the server must be fully down first, then restarted, then warmed with ONE real page load before any browser attaches. · **DEFERRED with reasons, not silently:** RC25 (capped mip chain) — the transparent-border bleed needs hand-built levels judged in a browser A/B at a 4-tile junction, and getting it wrong ships the seam grid the charter warns about. RC18/RC19/RC20/RC21 — with one design finding banked for RC19: **half-rate is NOT viable**, because `composer.render()` overwrites the PiP rect every frame, so the second pass needs a render target plus a textured-quad blit (RT linear, blit material `toneMapped: false`, map `colorSpace` LinearSRGB — that path reproduces the current look exactly). - **2026-08-25d · RENDERING CHARTER, GROUP C SHIPPED (RC6–RC11) — the seat/height core, and M5 came back a REFUTATION.** Gates: **vitest 1,996/1,996 (137 files, +85 over the session)** · astro check 0 err / 0 warn / 5 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · `verify-ultra.mjs` 29/29 · `verify-eclipse.mjs` 38/38 · `verify-rendering-charter.mjs` **56/56 ALL PASS**. Tier: LOCAL + BROWSER. · **THE HEADLINE IS M5, THE MEASUREMENT THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO ORDER GROUP D — AND IT KILLED ONE OF ITS SLICES.** The fidelity audit's §1.3 could not tell a QUADRATIC seat error (F1: both bakers project onto a tangent plane and never subtract the `d²/2R` curvature rise) from a FLAT BIAS (F2: the DSM includes the buildings it seats) or a TIME-DECAYING one (F4: streaming), and asked for "applied seat delta vs distance from the bake origin". **The first implementation of that histogram measured the wrong quantity twice before it measured the right one, and each wrong version looked completely plausible.** Binning the per-FEATURE applied delta gives a flat ~15 m rms at every distance — but that is WITHIN-CELL RELIEF, not bake error, because the per-cell seat absorbs the bake's error before any feature is placed. Binning `terrainSeat − bakedHeight` gives a clean +109 → +149 m growth that reads exactly like a quadratic — but the enriched bake is deliberately laid at h≈0 (`ENRICHED.reseatToTerrain`), so that number is the terrain height, not an error. **The quantity that actually answers the question is the curvature residual the per-cell re-seat CANNOT absorb: `d · cellHalfSpan / R`, because every cell is independently re-seated onto terrain at its own centre, so the tangent-plane rise survives only as its VARIATION ACROSS ONE CELL — bounded by the CELL radius, never the bake radius.** Browser-measured over the shipped Dnipro bake: at the 3,500–4,000 m ring, **0.568 m of curvature residual against 14.20 m rms of within-cell relief — 4.0 %.** **F1 IS REFUTED AS THE DOMINANT FLOATING MECHANISM, and RC12 (tangent-plane curvature subtraction, a re-bake of THREE regions) is not worth its cost.** Group D's value is RC13/RC15/RC16/RC17. The derivation is in `debugSeats()` next to the numbers so it can be re-derived rather than remembered (`ENRICHED.cellHalfSpanM`). · **RC6 (audit gap #2) — `lib/globe/terrainPick.ts`: the terrain samplers pick the FINEST hit, not the nearest.** `TilesFadePlugin` keeps a coarse parent in the scene and RAYCASTABLE for the whole crossfade after its children land, and over relief it can sit above the fine mesh — so `intersectObjects(...)[0]` seated buildings on the LOD error until the fade ended, then silently corrected. Tile depth is stamped at `load-model` from `tile.internal.depth`; `heightAt` and `pickGround` both select on it. **Measurement M7's answer is a NEGATIVE, and it is worth recording: over 37,742 samples at rest AND 9,874 more taken through an active refine flown in from 30 km, `hitsPerSample` was exactly 1.00 and the parent won ZERO times.** The mechanism is real in principle and does not occur at Dnipro; RC6 is cheap correctness insurance, not the fix for anything observed. · **RC11 (audit S16) — `lib/globe/heightMemo.ts`: an EXACT (epoch, lat, lon) memo in front of `heightAt`.** Not a spatial approximation — the seat sweep is a round-robin over a fixed set of footprints, so after one wrap it re-asks identical coordinates forever, and the correct cache introduces no new error term anywhere near the one-vertical-authority contract. Invalidation rides `terrainEpoch` (the BEST SPOT tile-load counter already living next to `heightAt`), so a hit is exactly as fresh as a raycast; `null` is deliberately never cached. Browser-measured: **84.0 % hit rate, 18,457 entries, 0 overflows** — which is what funded raising `reseatFeatureSamplesPerFrame` 16 → 64 and the tree budget 10 → 40. · **RC7 (audit S4) — look-biased seat priority + a never-sampled-first drain.** The sweep spent half its budget on the single nearest cell by pure distance, which in FPV is the cell under your feet, not the ones down the street you are looking at; cells are now ranked with the SAME bias law the download queue uses (`lookBiasedDistance`, one definition, so the seating front follows the streaming front) and features with no seat at all drain before any refresh. **A defect introduced and caught by the browser run in the same session: the drain popped an index and re-pushed it on failure, and since `pop()` takes from the same end, one unanswerable footprint retried immediately and forever, eating the whole budget.** Deferred indices now go to the BACK of the queue. Measured: look-cone convergence 50.3 % vs 33.9 % city-wide — the prioritisation is real, and the audit's S4 bar (>0.9 in the cone within 5 s) is **NOT met and recorded as an open tail**: the drain is deferring and the next step is instrumenting why (unloaded terrain under the footprint vs the gate — 4,570 gate rejections are counted, null-terrain deferrals are not). · **RC8 (audit gap #5) — the plausibility gate is relief-scaled and, for the first time, VISIBLE.** The flat 45 m bound was sized for Dnipro's ±20 m cells; in steep terrain every real sample exceeds it, and a gate rejecting 100 % of a cell's samples is indistinguishable on screen from a cell nobody has swept. The bound now widens from the relief a cell has ACTUALLY SHOWN (accepted samples only, so a garbage raycast can never widen the gate that would have caught it) and `debugSeats().rejected` publishes the gate's own work. · **RC9 (audit S6) — the seat cache that survives an LRU eviction.** Keyed by the baked content URI (the env-invariant identity U8's persisted overrides already use); feature seats keyed by baked feature id, so a re-bake simply misses. **Distance alone does not evict — the whole 6 km Dnipro bake fits inside the shipped desktop LRU cap, so at tier `high` these cells are never dropped, and the first version of this check reported 0 banked / 0 warm and looked exactly like a pass.** Squeezing the cache proves the banking leg (101 → 95 cells, 6 banked). The WARM-RESTORE leg is unit-covered but **browser-UNVERIFIED** — the evicted cells did not stream back inside the run's wait; it needs a leg that waits on `load-model` rather than on a timer. Open tail. · **RC10 (audit gap #8) — the FPV walk re-seat.** A temp FPV eye is `pinGround + up·eyeM + walkOffset`, and `walkOffset` is a fixed WORLD displacement — so the eye keeps the height of the ground under the PIN however far you walk, and `fpvEyeAboveGroundM` reported the nominal height either way. The ground under the WALKED eye is now sampled on DISTANCE (`FPV.walkReseatDistM` 4 m — standing still costs nothing, a sprint samples at a fixed spatial cadence whatever the frame rate) and eased with the same `seatStep` every other seat uses. Browser-measured over a 302 m walk: the correction tracked to 0.50 m, the largest camera-altitude change between samples was 0.23 m, and `__globe.u2()` recorded **zero >0.5 m single-frame eye jumps outside walk input**. · NEW DEV seams: `__globe.terrainPickStats()` / `resetTerrainPickStats()` / `heightMemoStats()`; `debugSeats()` gained `rejected`, `unseated`, `nearFeatures`/`nearFeaturesSampled`/`priorityCells`, `seatCacheHits`/`Misses`/`Cells` and `m5`; `__globe.fpv()` gained `walkGroundM`/`walkAppliedM`/`walkOffsetM`. - **2026-08-25c · RENDERING CHARTER, GROUP B: ALL FOUR OWNER BUGS FIXED AND BROWSER-PROVEN (RC1–RC5), plus RC0's cheap probes.** Gates: **vitest 1,970/1,970 (135 files, +59)** · astro check 0 err / 0 warn / 5 hints · `npx knip` exit-0 · `verify-ultra.mjs` 29/29 · `verify-eclipse.mjs` 38/38 · **NEW `scripts/verify-rendering-charter.mjs` 41/41 ALL PASS** (shots `charter-01..06`). Tier: LOCAL + BROWSER. **THE HEADLINE IS THAT THE HARNESS LIED FIRST.** The first run of the new script reported the sunset scrub as perfectly smooth — 61 samples, `max |d shadowIntensity| = 0.0000` — and it was measuring a FROZEN TAB: the verify Chrome carries no occlusion flags, rAF was throttled, and every scalar the rig writes per frame was the same stale frame's value, 61 times. A "no step" result from a page that never rendered a second frame is the most convincing possible pass and means nothing. Every sample now goes through `ticks()` (bringToFront + a real rAF count), and the script asserts that consecutive samples carry DISTINCT sun elevations — a zero-result validation on its own probe. Two more harness lies died with it: `bodies()` publishes `sunDir`, not `sunAltDeg`, so a bisection on `undefined > 0` walked silently to its own lower bound and "found sunset" at exactly the search start; and `JSON.stringify(<async IIFE>)` stringifies the PROMISE, so the M6 probe reported `{}` rather than failing. · **RC1 (B2, the square at totality) — `scene/glsl.impostorEdgeWindow` + `SKY.sunQuadFade [0.72, 0.98]`.** Two additive terms had no compact support at the sun quad's own boundary (the corona's `0.42·x^-2.6`, ≈0.003 linear there) and `DITHER_GLSL` painted ±1/256 over EVERY pixel of the plane including where the signal was exactly zero — truncating both at a straight edge IS a rectangle, readable only once the background fell to `ECLIPSE.daylightFloor`. Fix: one radial window closing to EXACTLY zero at 0.98 × `sunGlowExtent` = 6.86 of the plane's 7 inscribed disc radii, `discard` past it (the corners reach 9.9), and — the load-bearing detail — the window multiplies the colour **AFTER** the dither, so the noise fades out with the signal instead of stopping at a line; gating the dither would only have moved the straight edge onto the gate. Sibling pass on `skyTarget.ts` (`SKY_TARGET.quadFade [0.92, 0.995]`, chosen to clear the widest reticle tick the code can draw, 0.908 of the half-extent). Browser-measured at the owner's own Burgos totality hash: quad half-extent 210.4 css px, largest single-pixel luminance step across the old boundary **2.66 of 255 vs 32.05 in the control band inside it**, and 23.2 vs 24.33 either side. · **RC2 (B3, the sunset snap) — `lib/globe/keyHandoff.ts` + `SHADOWS.fadeBandSin 0.0523` (≈3°).** Three flips landed on the SAME boolean at sun elevation +0.46°: `castShadow`, the sun→moon key teleport, and the dedicated moonlight standing down — and the golden bell is flat at 1.0 there, so a kilometres-long 0.75-opacity shadow field vanished in one frame at the key's brightest moment of the day (2.025). The enabler is that `shadow.intensity` is a LIVE per-frame light uniform in three 0.185 (`LightShadow.js:41` → `WebGLLights.js:289` → `mix(1.0, shadow, shadowIntensity)`) AND `getShadowMask()` routes it through `ShadowMaterial` too, so ONE write fades the building-received shadows and the ground twins together. Both arms now trough to zero AT the gate and the dedicated moonlight gives up exactly the share the rig takes (`moonKs × (1 − moonRigTakeover)`), so the total moon key is continuous while the source changes underneath it. **The phantom night key is deliberately UNTOUCHED where no moon is waiting** — killing it is AB1, not this slice. Browser-measured across 103 samples from sun +4.21° to −1.28°: shadow field **1.000 → 0.0000 → 0.601**, largest single-step change **0.0270** (the old boolean was a 1.0 step), trough at sun elevation **0.426°** — the gate is asin(0.008) = 0.458° — with the key at 0.0000 there too, and `castShadow` never flips at all. · **RC3 (B4a) — the `!!focusHit` term is GONE from `shadowEligible`.** It survived the ULTRA rewrite verbatim and was the biggest shadow killer in FPV: any look at or above level nulls the ellipsoid intersection and one frame later `castShadow` was false AND every terrain caster was detached. Browser-proven: **4 of 7 sweep samples have no ellipsoid hit at all** (the fit equals `sqrt(h(2R+h))` at that sample's own altitude, 16.4–33.4 km) **and still cast**. · **RC4 (B4b) — `lib/globe/shadowFit.ts` + `SHADOWS.viewFitK 1` / `boundsQuantM 128`, and a rig-only `_shadowFocus`.** The ortho box was centred on the screen-centre ellipsoid hit with a half-extent riding altitude — but the hit distance rides altitude too, so whether your own foreground was inside the box depended on PITCH ALONE: `atan(1/boundsAltK)` = **59.0° base / 42.0° ULTRA, at every altitude**, which is why zooming never helped. The box is now framed on the VIEW (extent `clamp(max(alt·K, d/2 + boundsM/2), boundsM, cap)`, centre pushed `min(d/2, half − boundsM/2)` along the horizontal look) and `_focus` is untouched — that vector is the tilt/heading pivot and the lat/lon source for PLAN/FIND/BEST SPOT, and its documented miss-fallback is load-bearing for them. Browser-measured: the eye is inside the box at every pitch, the extent tracks the view (1,600 → 5,000 m across the sweep, quantized so `updateProjectionMatrix` still runs on change only), and at the owner's −0.2° case the box is 5,000 m with its centre 4,200 m ahead of the eye at 2.44 m per shadow texel. **The crispness trade, stated:** near-level looks now spend texels on 5 km instead of 1.6 km (0.78 → 2.44 m/texel) — but at those pitches there were NO shadows at all before RC3, and steeper looks keep 0.78–0.91. `viewFitK: 0` restores the old extent while keeping the re-centring; goes to the owner with AB4. · **RC5 (B1, "Map data not available") — `lib/globe/esriPlaceholder.ts` + a `public/sw.js` carve-out.** Esri answers **200 OK** outside its coverage with a 2,521-byte JPEG, byte-identical everywhere (re-probed live 2026-08-25 at Everest z19, a random Pacific z19 and a z22 past the service's max level: same bytes, md5 `f27d9de7f80c13501f470595e327aa6d`, ETag `"vvvvvvvvvvvvf"`). Every fallback in the stack is a network-FAILURE path, so none of them ever armed. A construction-time `o.fetch` wrapper now detects the sentinel, fetches the parent, crops this tile's quadrant and upscales it — the placeholder never draws — plus a once-per-tile `cache: "default"` heal (against `force-cache` pinning) and a learned cap table that skips known-sentinel GETs. **THE CHARTER'S PROPOSED DETECTOR WOULD HAVE HALF-FAILED IN THE BROWSER: Esri sends no `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`, so `ETag` is not CORS-readable from a page** (`res.headers.get("etag")` is null) — detection is on the BYTES, length + FNV-1a-32 `0x92d9118f`, pinned against a committed fixture. Browser-proven against the LIVE service through a DEV probe (`__globe.esriProbe`), because whether a camera pose ever asks for an out-of-coverage tile depends on the terrain tileset's own LOD there: three real Everest sentinels came back as **6,790 / 12,618 / 11,441 bytes of real imagery**, the summit tile passed through untouched at 11,556, repeat GETs were all skipped without re-asking the service, and the one case the fallback cannot answer (past Esri's max level, where every ancestor is a sentinel too) fails soft and is then served from a banked body rather than re-GET forever. · **RC0 probes recorded:** M1 FPV `near` **1.0 m** / `far` **180,375 m** (ratio 180,375) · M2 **DEPTH_BITS 24**, webgl2 · M6 `heightAt` **0.018–0.067 ms/sample**, 200/200 answered over a loaded city · M8 Dnipro terrain 68.9–186.0 m. M5 (the §1.3 separator ordering Group C vs D) still open — it needs per-cell seat instrumentation that Group C opens anyway. Reusing the shipped directional shadow map: 7 separate breakers (one shadow light, latched `mapSize`, a 2nd `DirectionalLight` forces a full material recompile and darkens every ground `ShadowMaterial` twin, terrain does not cast outside ULTRA, and the rig itself refuses below 0.46° — the hero cases are 0–2°). **OWNER RULINGS 2026-08-24: R1** DRONE semantics above **5 m** (below it the ground mask gates; above it only solid INTERIORS), with a `groundReachable` bit carried alongside because the owner's stated preference was "a place I can climb to" · **R2** FPV is a CENTRE SOURCE but renders NOTHING in the viewfinder (§9 records the exact three changes to un-park it) · **R3** field at **3 m**, **1 m reserved for ULTRA**, shortlist always re-solved at 1 m · **R4** GL overlay only, radial rim falloff, and **the hollow cylinder is REPLACED by a centre PLUMB LINE** + billboarded altitude chip + ground tick. **THE METRIC** (§3): `S = A_hard · A_soft^0.5 · M · G(V) · [0.15V + 0.30L + 0.25P + 0.30F]`, gates multiply and preferences sum, `G(V)` SOFT because the hero case intentionally occults the disc, coverage < 0.5 ⇒ **UNKNOWN, no ink** (never a cold colour). converted once at the track via astronomy-engine's exported `Refraction("normal", alt)` (verified at `astronomy.js:6361`, agrees with `Horizon(...,"normal")` to 1e-12), and the two refractions still never mix. **THE PLAN WAS WRONG IN FIFTEEN PLACES and every one is now recorded in its AS BUILT appendix** — the notch floor read the WALL not the gap (`Hg(az*)` samples the flank BY CONSTRUCTION: depth 0, `F_notch` ≡ 0 in `cellScore` while the standalone kernel returned 0.358; Terrain-only entries go at the registry TAIL (`BAKED_REGIONS[0]` is the last-resort fallback and its `variants[0]` is read unguarded — both pinned). **(3) THREE MEASURED FINDINGS, all recorded in BAKED_ASSETS §2/§3/§6.** **(a) THE VERIFICATION REFERENCE WAS WRONG, NOT THE BAKE.** The first Everest bake failed its own probe with a −39.3 m "datum fault". `geoid.mjs` held ONE grid (lons 34–36 / lats 48–49) and **CLAMPED** out-of-range lookups to its nearest corner, so the Everest probe was answered with Dnipro's 36 E/48 N value, **+20.025 m where the truth is −28.341 m**. A correct bake was indicted by a reference extrapolating 5,000 km. The clamp is now a **throw**, the bake **pre-flights grid coverage before meshing**, and sampling a grid is step 2 of the add-a-city runbook. *A reference that extrapolates silently turns "no data" into evidence — and the evidence indicts the wrong component.* **(b) THE PROBE ONLY WORKED ON A PLAIN.** It compared ONE tile-centre sample against the source COG: on a river plain that is accuracy, in the Khumbu it is one point's slope error. Rewritten to a 9×9 interior grid splitting **bias** (median signed — the datum gate, which cancels sign-symmetric slope error however steep the ground) from **spread** (median absolute — the resolution gate, the only one that must grow with relief), plus a positive control that all 81 samples land inside a source COG so a mosaic at −180/90 cannot pass with a median of nothing. Tolerances are region-scoped with the Dnipro numbers as defaults. NEW `--probe-only` re-runs verification against an on-disk tree writing nothing — **the only safe way to re-touch an existing bake, because the rim blend is not idempotent** — and it re-verified the SHIPPED Dnipro artifact unchanged: bias +0.4 m / spread 2.4 m. Everest: bias +8.8 m / spread 10.2 m on a probe tile carrying **1,944 m of relief**, rim Δ **−0.0 m**. **(c) MESH INTENSITY IS NOT THE LEVER — measured and REJECTED.** mago's `--intensity` (default 4, never set by the bake) at 12 tripled vertices (1,096 → 3,500) and bytes (21 → 68 KB) and moved the summit sample by **0.1 m**. The shipped default is already at the source's information limit; raising it would take 210 MB to ~640 MB for nothing. **What the height map can actually do, end to end:** GLO-30 reads the summit pixel at **8,732 m** — the 30 m DSM is **111 m below the published 8,848.86 m apex by construction** — our mesh carries that peak to within 3 m (tile max 8,734.9 m vs the source's 8,737.8 m window max), and a point sample at the apex coordinate reads lower still because a 34 m posting cannot resolve a pyramid. **The bake is faithful to its source; the source is not faithful to the apex.** Also REJECTED with its cost: dropping `extentMaxDepth` below `maxDepth` would cut 210 MB → ~41 MB but moves the patch↔CWT boundary off the rim-blended extent edge onto an un-blended `cityBbox` edge — 170 MB bought with a height cliff ringing the region. **(4) FPV far-field accuracy investigation ran READ-ONLY** (owner: "do not implement anything in this session") — six-dimension inventory + adversarial refutation; report and slice ladder recorded separately. - **2026-08-22b · OWNER FOLLOW-UP (/m PiP re-seat) + FOUR AUDIT-#3 DEFECTS IN 22a FIXED BEFORE SHIP + the auto-ship title trap CLOSED.** **(A) Owner order: the /m PiP moves up so its TOP aligns with the MAP/+/− pills.** Done structurally rather than by matching literals — NEW `--mw-top-y 0.6rem` + `--mw-pip-h 32dvh` tokens (`map-window.css`) that `.mw-top`, `body.m .mw-pip` and `body.m .mw-recenter` all derive from, so the whole `[+ −] · [PiP] · [◉]` right rail moves as one if the rung ever changes. Browser-measured: PiP top **9.59 px** == row top == pill top; pills end at 140 px, the PiP starts at 253 px (no horizontal overlap); `.mw-top` stays `pointer-events:none` so its full-width invisible box passes taps through. **(B) REGRESSION the move exposed, fixed:** the PiP is a *hole* (draw() clearRects it, `body.m .mw` has no background), so anything fixed between the GL canvas (z 0) and the map window (z 20) paints INSIDE it — the batch-#5 "minimap inside minimap" class. The new rung put the hole over `.m-status` (Plux · account · GUIDE · DESKTOP, z 10) and the chips showed through. `.m-status` joins the existing `body.m.mw-open { visibility: hidden }` group — nothing is lost, the opaque map canvas already covered the strip. The **z-2 DOM label layers** (`.sky-names` ×2 modules, `.geo-labels`, `.bldg-edit-label`) joined too: that bleed is **PRE-EXISTING** (the hole overlapped the upper screen at 3.4rem as well) and was surfaced, not caused, by the new **intersection sweep** the verify suite now runs — it enumerates every fixed 1 ≤ z < 20 surface overlapping the PiP rect instead of naming today's offenders, so the next reseat cannot silently reintroduce the class. Those layers are positioned for the FULL-SCREEN view while the hole shows a scaled miniature, so they could only ever paint in the wrong place there. **(C) AUDIT #3 wave 1 (Track A1) found three MAJORs in the 22a slice itself — all fixed before ship, not deferred:** **A1-1** — `panBy()` latched on EVERY pointer move, so the ~2 px drift of a 500 ms long-press (the primary /m chart gesture — it places a point) armed the now-permanent override, with ◉ the only way out. The latch moved into `onPointerMove` behind the SAME `DRAG_CANCEL_PX 6` threshold that already decides drag-from-press; sub-threshold moves still pan (the follow tidies them) but never latch. Browser-verified: a 3 px jitter leaves the button unlit. **A1-2** — the ◉ button, the sole exit from the permanent override, **slid under the FPV altitude column on short viewports**: `.m-altcol` is lifted to z 24 by `body.mw-open`, ABOVE the z-20 window, so the tap would have nudged the eye's altitude instead. (Raising the button's z cannot fix this — `.mw` is itself a z-20 stacking context.) `.m-altcol`'s geometry is now published as `--m-altcol-bottom` + `--m-altcol-h` and `body.m .mw-recenter`'s top is a `min()` whose second arm is the last rung clearing that box. Verified at three viewports: 390×844 keeps the PiP rung (287.7 px, alt at 485.6), 360×640 → 222.4 vs 281.6, **360×560 → the floor engages at 160 px vs alt at 201.6** (before the fix it was 86% occluded). **A1-3** — the desktop attribution truncated below ≈900 px: the `clamp()` FLOOR (6.4 px) stops the shrink while the 265-char list keeps its width, and `nowrap` + the bar's `overflow:hidden` clipped BOTH ends — "© Esri" itself — against the rule 22a had just written down and the T17 ToS posture. A `@media (max-width: 60rem)` branch wraps the line and redefines `--mw-credit-h 1.8rem`, so `time-scrubber.css`'s lift tracks it with no second rule. Verified unclipped at 1100 / 820 / 700 px with the scrubber lifting 48.8 → 64.0 px. **A1-15 (MINOR)** — `.tr` (TimeReadout) is deliberately co-axial with the scrub rail but was not lifted, splitting the two instruments by exactly `--mw-credit-h`; `body.mw-open .tr` now takes the same lift (separation measured **10.4 px**, the designed 0.65 rem). **(D) SUPERSEDES a 22a claim (audit A1-13):** "◉ is the one and only path back" is not literally true — `manualPan` and `setPanned` both live in the `[open]` effect, so closing and reopening the window also clears the latch (on /m the PiP tap = close, so an accidental pinch-latch is one tap from clearing). The code comments and the guide copy say so now. **(E) AUTO-SHIP TITLE TRAP CLOSED (owner report):** the 2026-08-22 00:20 ship pushed `claude/ship-20260822-002057` fine but **no PR was ever created** — GitHub rejects an over-long PR title and the automation derives it from the commit subject, which was **1,221 chars**. `.claude/hooks/session-end-ship.sh` now caps the SUBJECT at `SUBJECT_MAX=200` bytes, rewinds to the last word boundary (which also discards any byte-split multibyte char), appends `…`, logs the cap, and puts the **full untruncated title in the commit body** so nothing is lost. Exercised against the 1,184-char title: 198 chars / 202 bytes out, valid UTF-8, 225 with the ` #pr #skipreview #automerge` tags — under GitHub's 256 limit. - **2026-08-22a · OWNER MICRO-SLICE SHIPPED 3/3 (the addendum below, implemented) — expanded-map manual-pan override made PERMANENT + ◉ RE-CENTRE button + bottom-edge attribution.** **(1) The manual-pan override is now permanent (SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-21g eye-motion re-arm).** Owner ruling after device-testing the slice: *"I do not want to auto-latch back after any manual panning starts, at all."* `MapWindow.tsx` loses `FOLLOW_REARM_M`, the `manualAnchor` ref, the anchor capture in `latchManualPan()` and the whole re-arm block at the top of `draw()`; `manualPan` is still armed by `panBy()` (drag + pinch midpoint) and by the 2-pointer branch of `onPointerDown` (zoom/twist-only pinches), wheel/chip zoom still never latches, and the `&& !manualPan` guard on the FPV-follow block stands. Walking now leaves the chart exactly where it was dragged **even once the eye leaves the visible bounds** — browser-measured: eye **302 m** from the chart centre vs a **184 m** half-diagonal, chart moved **0.0 m**. The radar/cone/eye-dot rule needed NO code change (they are already placed at `toPx(anchor)` → `xformNow().fwd`, so they scroll off with the world) — VERIFIED rather than edited, as ordered. **(2) NEW round ◉ RE-CENTRE button** (`.mw-btn.mw-recenter`, U+25C9, `aria-label="Centre the map on me"`), right edge under the top row at the rung item 3 freed (`right .75rem / top 3.1rem`; on **/m** it drops BELOW the 32vw×32dvh PiP that owns that rung — `top: calc(3.4rem + 32dvh + .5rem + safe-top)`, a deviation from the spec's literal seat because the PiP would have collided). Action = `aimAnchorNow(...)` (the SAME anchor the radar uses) → write `view.current.lat/lonDeg` → clear the latch → `requestRedraw()`; wired through the existing `zoomButtons.current` bridge idiom as `recenterRef.current`. The latch is mirrored into React **on transitions only** (`if (manualPan) return` in `latchManualPan`; a fence test asserts `draw()` contains no `setPanned`) — never per pan event, never per frame. Owner-flagged taste call, done: always visible, muted while following, **accent-lit** while overridden. Measured: ◉ centres to **0.0 m** from the eye, following resumes and re-tracks to the **18.5 m** deadband edge. **(3) ALL map attribution → ONE very thin full-bleed line on the SCREEN's bottom edge, under the time strip.** New `--mw-credit-h 0.85rem`; the two bottom-anchored time surfaces are **lifted by it, never z-bumped** — `body.mw-open .ts { bottom: calc(2.2rem + var(--mw-credit-h)) }` and `body.m.mw-open .m-bottom { bottom: calc(var(--mw-credit-h) + env(safe-area-inset-bottom)) }` (the dock's mw-open `padding-bottom` drops its safe-area term — the credit bar owns the inset now, `box-sizing: content-box` so the inset ADDS to the line height). `.mw-creditbar` is a **SIBLING of `.mw`**, not a child: `.mw` carries the centring/drag transform, which would be the containing block for any `position:fixed` descendant and trap the bar inside the desktop window. `pointer-events:none` on the bar, `auto` on the anchor only; `white-space: nowrap` with viewport-scaled type (`clamp`) so the contractual source list never truncates. **Shell split discovered in browser verification (a real collision the spec did not anticipate):** on desktop `index.astro`'s page-level `.map-credit` ALREADY pins an attribution line to the bottom edge whose source list is a strict **superset** of the map window's (Cesium ion · OpenMapTiles · Copernicus · NASA · Gaia on top of Esri·CARTO·OSM) — the new bar drew straight over it (two overlapping lines, first desktop shot). Resolution: `.mw-creditbar` is **/m-only** (the `.mw-pip` precedent — /m has no page credit at all) and desktop instead **promotes the page line** to the same full-bleed bottom bar while `body.mw-open`, via a new inert `.map-creditbar` wrapper (`pointer-events:none`; the anchor re-enables itself on its BASE rule, so the link stays clickable in BOTH states — the closed-state check caught that regression). Result on both shells: exactly ONE bottom line, unclipped, with the scrubber/dock riding above it. **Verify + supersede (house rule):** `scripts/verify-qaslice-cab.mjs`'s *"A: walking re-armed the follow"* check is **annotated as superseded and inverted**, plus new checks for the off-bounds hold, the ◉ button (present · lit · centres · unlights · following resumed) and the attribution bar; a **DESKTOP leg** was added to that script (it was /m-only) covering the ◉ seat, the mouse-drag latch, the promoted page bar and the closed-state restore. `scripts/verify-uxbatch7.mjs`'s *"credit re-seated to the top band"* check is likewise annotated + inverted to bottom-anchored. NEW `test/styles/mapWindowChrome.test.ts` (11 tests, the swTileCache/hiddenPairs source-fence idiom) locks the cross-file contract: no re-arm survives, `manualPan = false` has exactly two sites (declaration + ◉), the transition-only mirror, the sibling-not-descendant bar, the `--mw-credit-h` lift in all three files, the no-double-inset rule, the round-button geometry and the /m PiP clearance.
